# Changelog

## 2026-08-23

### Fixed

- **Recommendation generation no longer proposes work already covered by an existing Action.** Luna now receives a structured `activeWork` ledger of all proposed and in-progress Actions, including their operation types, target resource IDs, and diagnosed problems. A server-side novelty gate (operation + target fingerprint comparison) runs before persistence and rejects exact duplicates and partial overlaps without relying on prose-matching. Previously, three near-identical "hide out-of-stock products" Actions could appear simultaneously because generation ran against stale memory with no awareness of current Action state; now the snapshot hash includes active work, so a new Action immediately changes what future generation runs see. `app/lib/shopify/agentic-runtime/{action-fingerprint,recommendation-service,recommendation-agent}.server.js`, `tests/recommendation-novelty.test.mjs`.

- **Proposal generation now observes completion automatically — no manual refresh needed.** The worker was overwriting `sourceMode` to `"agentic"` when picking up a home-triggered job, causing the polling filter to lose track of the run mid-flight. The UI froze at "Finding your next move…" and the completed proposal only appeared after a browser refresh. The worker no longer touches `sourceMode`, so home-triggered runs stay filterable throughout their lifecycle. A stuck-run threshold (15 min of no DB activity) now surfaces as a retryable failure rather than a permanent spinner (`app/lib/shopify/agentic-runtime/recommendation-service.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-plan/home-proposal-generation.server.js`, `tests/home-proposal-generation.test.mjs`).

- **"Generate a proposal" now reliably shows the generating state and surfaces a result.** A sourceMode mismatch (`"home"` vs `"agentic"`) meant the in-flight and daily-cap checks always returned zero — so after clicking the button, the loader immediately reported `isGenerating: false` and the button reappeared as if nothing happened. Home-triggered runs are now stored with `sourceMode: "home"`, the loader correctly detects in-flight jobs and polls until they complete, and the card shows an explicit message when the last run found no executable opportunity (`app/lib/shopify/agentic-runtime/recommendation-service.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-plan/home-proposal-generation.server.js`, `app/components/daily-home.tsx`, `app/routes/app._index.tsx`, `tests/home-proposal-generation.test.mjs`).

- **Accepted Agentic Actions no longer cause HTTP 524 timeouts.** The full post-acceptance execution loop (Luna planning, Shopify reads/writes, verification) previously ran synchronously inside the merchant's request, routinely exceeding Cloudflare's ~100 s origin-response limit. Repeated 524s then created concurrent server-side loops that could diverge on the accepted-revision guard and block collection writes. Acceptance now returns immediately after persisting `acceptedActionRevision` and enqueueing a `agentic_shopify_execute:<actionId>` job in the existing BackfillJob worker; execution runs in the background with the same Luna + Shopify gateway path. One job per (Action, accepted revision): repeated "go ahead" messages for the same revision are idempotent — the existing queued/running job is returned. A material replan while a queued job exists cancels the stale job before the revision advances. `accepted` in the Action lifecycle now correctly means `acceptedActionRevision === currentActionRevision`, not merely that any prior acceptance exists. `app/lib/shopify/agentic-runtime/{execution-job,execution-service,action-chat,constants}.server.js`, `app/lib/actions/action-command.server.js`, `app/services/{shopify-backfill-worker,shopify-backfill-status}.server.js`, `tests/agentic-execution-job.test.mjs`.

- **Agentic execution jobs no longer race in multi-process deployments.** A RUNNING BackfillJob that was upserted to QUEUED for a new revision while the old worker was still executing could be claimed by a second worker, letting two `runAgenticShopifyExecution` loops run simultaneously for the same Action row. The enqueue path now refuses to reset a RUNNING job; instead it writes `pendingAcceptedRevision` into the payload. The running worker checks for this field on completion and returns `{requeue: true}` with the updated payload, so the generic CAS transitions the row to QUEUED for the new revision in a single atomic step — one job slot, one active loop, always. `app/lib/shopify/agentic-runtime/execution-job.server.js`, `app/services/shopify-backfill-worker.server.js`, `tests/agentic-execution-job.test.mjs`.

## 2026-08-22

### Fixed

- **Context answers now resume the first-value learning pipeline instead of freezing on “Still working”.** After bootstrap finished first, `answerOnboardingContext` called recommendation queueing, got `missing_completed_goals`, and created no job. The loader then treated `fullLearning.state === "learning"` as proof of work. There is now one idempotent `ensureOnboardingLearningProgress` orchestrator that inspects durable state and queues the next missing stage — store understanding, insights, goals, then the agentic recommendation. Loader polls self-heal an unscheduled pipeline before responding, and “Still working” requires a queued or running relevant job or recommendation run. `app/lib/onboarding/{learning-progress,fast-onboarding}.server.js`, `app/components/fast-value-onboarding.tsx`, `tests/onboarding-learning-progress.test.mjs`.

- **Agentic recommendation semantic repair no longer burns the investigation loop.** Luna was producing a valid “In Stock collection” idea, then `validatePromiseConsistency` rejected the missing `available` criterion and the same investigation-loop repair ran until six iterations stored `BLOCKED`. Structured eligibility is now primary in the recommendation contract; a consistency mismatch gets one focused repair (add the supported criterion, or remove the unsupported qualifier) and then persists or terminates as `VALIDATION_FAILED`. `validatePromiseConsistency` is unchanged. `app/lib/shopify/agentic-runtime/{recommendation-agent,eligibility}.server.js`, `tests/agentic-eligibility.test.mjs`.

- **Memory rebuild now retires vanished deterministic metrics after the provenance fix.** Shopify-derived facts now persist as `directObservation`, but rebuild still only obsoleted `systemInference` rows. When inventory (or another source) disappears, inferred `directObservation` beliefs are now retired the same way, while merchant-confirmed/corrected beliefs stay untouched. `app/lib/merchant-memory/service.server.js`, `tests/merchant-memory.test.mjs`.

### Added

- **Agentic Actions now carry first-class eligibility criteria, separate from write protections.** Recommendations must emit structured predicates for who qualifies (status, product type, inventory, tags, price, and other Shopify-state fields) instead of leaving those rules only in prose. Hydration copies them onto the semantic Action; they participate in revision identity; Action Chat can add a merchant rule or restore the original criteria from revision history; execution re-reads current Shopify state and refuses writes to now-ineligible resources; verification uses the same predicates. A consistency check rejects merchant-facing “in-stock” / high-margin wording that is missing from the structured contract, and the opposite case remains valid: an Action that promises all active Wine Bundles does not invent an inventory rule. `app/lib/shopify/agentic-runtime/{eligibility,semantic-action,recommendation-agent,action-chat,execution-agent,recommendation-service}.server.js`, `tests/agentic-eligibility.test.mjs`, `GLOSSARY.md`.

### Changed

- **Agentic recommendation runs now keep a server-owned investigation ledger and converge to a valid Action or an explicit blocker instead of dying on protocol repair.** Each turn receives `investigationState` (`satisfiedRequirements`, `investigationComplete`, `lastCandidate`, `lastValidationError`) so completed Shopify reads survive later validation failures. Identical successful reads in the same immutable run return `ALREADY_AVAILABLE` instead of re-executing; different arguments still run. Validation errors now name the exact field, invalid values, allowed IDs and a one-step `repairInstruction` without restarting investigation. `diagnosedProblem` / `mechanism` requirements are unchanged. Live frozen wine snapshot: 18/20 completed Actions, 2/20 explicit evidence-based blockers, 0/20 iteration limits (was 6/10 completed). Strong-category counterfactual 5/5 inventory correction; positive merchandising 5/5 collection when a discovery gap is evidenced. 30 convergence tests added. `app/lib/shopify/agentic-runtime/recommendation-agent.server.js`, `tests/recommendation-convergence.test.mjs`, `.context/agentic-anchoring/run-diagnostic.mjs`, `docs/diagnostics/agentic-recommendation-runtime-convergence.md`.

- **Agentic recommendations must now identify a specific Shopify-state gap and its causal mechanism before an action can be selected.** Two required fields added to the recommendation schema: `diagnosedProblem` (the concrete constraint in current Shopify state, distinct from commercial signal) and `mechanism` (why the proposed change directly addresses that constraint). Validation rejects recommendations that supply only commercial salience ("Wine Bundles = 22.93% of revenue") without identifying what Shopify state is wrong. System prompt updated with an explicit reasoning sequence: evidence → identified gap → mechanism → capability → action, and the principle that tool availability is a feasibility condition, not a justification. Live evaluation: all 6 completed wine-snapshot runs now cite specific Shopify gaps ("zero collections exist") confirmed by reads, vs. pure revenue percentages before; 17/17 strong-category counterfactual runs correctly selected inventory correction over unjustified collection creation; 5/5 positive-merchandising runs confirmed collections are still selected when a genuine discoverability gap is evidenced. `app/lib/shopify/agentic-runtime/recommendation-agent.server.js`, `app/lib/shopify/agentic-runtime/recommendation-service.server.js`, `.context/agentic-anchoring/run-diagnostic.mjs`, `tests/recommendation-mechanism.test.mjs`, `docs/diagnostics/action-mechanism-evidence-validation.md`.

- **Deterministic Shopify-derived facts now correctly land in `storeEvidence` rather than `jefeHypotheses`.** All 40 beliefs for the investigated wine merchant were being normalised as `system_inference` (precedence 20), including revenue-share percentages, inventory availability and order metrics that are mechanically calculated from Shopify data. The root cause was `shopify-derivations.server.js` hardcoding `BELIEF_PRECEDENCE.systemInference` instead of `directObservation`. Fix: source precedence corrected to `directObservation` (new rows); exported `authorityLevel` extended with an optional `evidence` parameter that promotes historical rows when their linked evidence carries `evidenceType: "deterministic_calculation"`. After the fix: 36 deterministic beliefs land in `storeEvidence` (was 0), 3 genuine inferences remain in `jefeHypotheses`, and runtime blocking dropped from 60% to 0% across 5 sanity-check runs. 13 regression tests added; all 33 provenance and candidate-bound tests still pass. `app/lib/merchant-memory/shopify-derivations.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-insights/candidates.server.js`, `app/lib/shopify/agentic-runtime/recommendation-service.server.js`, `.context/agentic-anchoring/run-diagnostic.mjs`, `tests/recommendation-deterministic-provenance.test.mjs`, `docs/diagnostics/deterministic-evidence-provenance-validation.md`.

## 2026-08-21

### Added

- **Jefe validated the provenance fix against 10 live Luna runs and 10 counterfactual runs; classification: provenance IMPROVED-BUT-REMAINS, collection tendency ACTION-SURFACE-BIASED.** Luna now explicitly qualifies cases/bundles as "unverified" / "needs_verification" rather than treating it as established merchant intent — the structural separation is working. The counterfactual (inventory-mismatch vs collection) showed 10/10 correct non-collection selections when evidence clearly contradicts merchandising. However, all 4 wine-store completions still selected collection without establishing a discoverability gap (action-surface bias confirmed). Runtime blocking rate also increased (6/10 vs 2/5 pre-fix) — a robustness follow-up is needed. `docs/diagnostics/merchant-intent-vs-jefe-inference-validation.md`, `.context/agentic-anchoring/post-fix-condition-a.json`, `.context/agentic-anchoring/post-fix-counterfactual.json`.

- **Luna now receives merchant intent, store evidence and Jefe hypotheses as structurally separate layers — not a single flat memory block.** The agentic recommendation snapshot splits `merchantMemory` into `merchantIntent` (raw onboarding statements + merchant-confirmed/corrected beliefs), `storeEvidence` (deterministic Shopify observations), and `jefeHypotheses` (LLM-generated goals, insights and inferred beliefs). Goals and insights now carry `generatedBy: "jefe_llm"` and `authority: "jefe_interpretation"`. Belief authority is now a human-readable string (`"deterministic"`, `"merchant_confirmed"`, `"system_inference"`, `"lower_authority_inference"`) instead of a raw integer — consistent with what the Insights and Goals prompts already used. The system prompt instructs Luna that the same hypothesis appearing in multiple Jefe-generated objects is still one underlying inference, not independent evidence, and that merchant confirmation is required to promote a hypothesis to merchant intent. Eleven unit tests cover provenance isolation for all five source classes. `app/lib/shopify/agentic-runtime/{recommendation-service,recommendation-agent}.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-insights/candidates.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-goals/candidates.server.js`, `tests/recommendation-provenance.test.mjs`, `docs/diagnostics/merchant-intent-vs-jefe-inference-validation.md`.

- **Jefe now has an upstream provenance diagnostic for Luna's Cases & Bundles recommendation.** The new report traces the recovered recommendation back through raw merchant intent, deterministic store evidence, Store Understanding, Insights and generated Goals; runs a five-by-five current-upstream vs raw-evidence-only A/B without writes; and classifies the behaviour as grounded amplification with upstream self-anchoring risk rather than pure merchant intent or hallucination. `docs/diagnostics/upstream-goal-insight-self-anchoring.md`.

- **Jefe now has a live diagnostic investigation of Luna's collection anchoring.** The new report reconstructs the recoverable agentic recommendation run that selected an in-stock Cases & Bundles collection, shows that `collectionCreate` was visible before Luna's first hypothesis, runs six-condition stability checks plus a counterfactual inventory fixture without persisting recommendations or Shopify writes, and classifies the behaviour as primarily evidence-driven with generated-goal self-anchoring risk. `docs/diagnostics/agentic-recommendation-anchoring-investigation.md`.

- **Jefe now has a runtime LLM and prompt audit for Luna.** The new diagnostic maps every production, background, multimodal and eval-only model path; traces model-visible prompts, retrieval, tool descriptions, conversation history and feedback loops; and flags where examples, generated Shopify API stubs or stored Luna outputs may anchor future behaviour before any runtime changes are made. `docs/diagnostics/llm-runtime-and-prompt-audit.md`.

- **Luna now has an end-to-end agentic Shopify runtime on the generated API surface.** The new recommendation loop gives Luna Merchant Memory, bounded store evidence, compact Shopify stubs, read tools and searchable API retrieval so it can investigate hypotheses before proposing a semantic Action. Accepted Actions now carry outcome, scope, constraints, expected material effects and a single `acceptedActionRevision`; after acceptance, Luna chooses generated Shopify reads/writes through the universal gateway, can retrieve more stubs mid-run, and must verify Shopify state after writes before completion. Deterministic golden paths cover an unseen multi-operation collection Action, a second unrelated product-metafield Action, no-write-before-acceptance, accepted-intent drift blocking, idempotent replay and unknown-result reconciliation. Live Luna/dev-Shopify validation is available behind the explicit evaluator flags and write gate. `app/lib/shopify/agentic-runtime/**`, `app/lib/shopify/api/{catalogs/shopify-admin-api-2026-07.generated.json,gateway.server.js}`, `scripts/{eval-agentic-shopify-runtime,eval-jefe-golden-path}.mjs`, `tests/{agentic-shopify-runtime,shopify-api-gateway}.test.mjs`, `docs/ops/agentic-shopify-runtime-v1.md`, `GLOSSARY.md`.

- **Jefe now has a universal Shopify operation gateway for the agentic runtime rebuild.** The new generated Admin GraphQL API stub catalogue, retrieval helper and gateway let Luna work from Shopify operations rather than Jefe feature executors: reads can run for investigation, mutations are denied unless a current accepted Action revision authorises them, variables and granted scopes are validated before provider calls, obvious intent drift is blocked, and every admitted or denied operation is written to the new `shopify_operation_calls` ledger. Existing typed adapters remain in place while the recommendation and execution agents migrate onto this substrate. `app/lib/shopify/api/**`, `scripts/shopify-api-generate.mjs`, `prisma/schema.prisma`, `prisma/migrations/20260821113000_shopify_operation_call_ledger/migration.sql`, `app/routes/health.tsx`, `tests/shopify-api-gateway.test.mjs`, `docs/ops/agentic-shopify-runtime-v1.md`, `GLOSSARY.md`.

### Changed

- **Agentic Shopify Action Chat now uses the generated Shopify API surface before acceptance.** Focused chat for `agentic_shopify` Actions now bypasses the legacy focused-action tool catalogue and gives Luna semantic Action read/update tools, Shopify operation retrieval and read-only gateway calls. Display milestones remain explanatory workspace context instead of becoming runnable `work[]` steps; scope and constraint refinements persist to the semantic draft revision; clear merchant acceptance creates `acceptedActionRevision` and hands off to the post-acceptance execution loop without a second capability approval. The runtime evaluator now also exports every captured live LLM prompt plus all agentic prompt templates to `.context/agentic-shopify-runtime/prompts-latest.json`. `app/lib/shopify/agentic-runtime/{action-chat,tools}.server.js`, `app/lib/actions/{action-state,action-command}.server.js`, `app/lib/actions/agent/focused-action-turn.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-memory/{conversation,general-chat}.server.js`, `app/routes/app._index.tsx`, `scripts/eval-agentic-shopify-runtime.mjs`, `tests/agentic-shopify-runtime.test.mjs`, `GLOSSARY.md`.

- **Browser onboarding now uses Luna's agentic Shopify recommendation runtime as the canonical first-move path.** Completing merchant goals now queues `agentic_recommendation_generate`; the worker runs Luna with Merchant Memory, bounded evidence, searchable Shopify API stubs and live Shopify reads, then persists one semantic recommendation and Merchant Action without pre-authored workflow steps or legacy `actionType` routing. Fast onboarding now surfaces only `sourceMode: "agentic"` recommendations, accepts the linked semantic Action revision, and executes through the agentic Shopify execution service. `app/lib/{merchant-goals/service,onboarding/fast-onboarding,merchant-plan/service}.server.js`, `app/lib/shopify/agentic-runtime/**`, `app/routes/app._index.tsx`, `app/services/{shopify-backfill-status,shopify-backfill-worker}.server.js`, `tests/merchant-goals.test.mjs`.

- **The old bootstrap recommendation generator and worker recommendation-review/reconciliation dispatch are retired from the active runtime.** `merchant_memory_bootstrap` now captures evidence and stops at `ready_for_agentic_recommendation`; legacy bootstrap alternative/reconciliation helpers return retired compatibility statuses, and the worker explicitly rejects active `merchant_plan_generate` and `recommendation_review` jobs instead of running the old recommendation architecture. `app/lib/onboarding/bootstrap.server.js`, `app/services/shopify-backfill-worker.server.js`.

- **The agentic Shopify runtime evaluator now proves the live dev-store path end to end.** It captures Luna's live-selected collection recommendation, materialises that same semantic Action for `jefe-local-store.myshopify.com`, accepts only the `acceptedActionRevision`, lets Luna execute through generated Shopify stubs and the universal gateway, verifies the final Shopify collection by read-back, records live write/recovery/idempotent-replay evidence, and reruns the dev merchant onboarding investigation to show Luna retrieving broader Shopify reads from real Merchant Memory. `scripts/eval-agentic-shopify-runtime.mjs`, `app/lib/shopify/agentic-runtime/{execution-agent,recommendation-agent,tools}.server.js`, `app/lib/shopify/api/gateway.server.js`, `tests/agentic-shopify-runtime.test.mjs`, `GLOSSARY.md`.

### Fixed

- **Completed agentic Shopify Actions now project their accepted outcome consistently into Action Chat and the workspace.** The shared Action state now carries the persisted accepted semantic revision, verified outcome and Shopify operation history, so Luna can answer post-completion questions from the existing read/write ledger instead of treating the Action as unaccepted or suggesting a retry. Completed agentic workspace milestones now render as historical plan context without pre-acceptance status chips, and the header no longer calls them execution steps. `app/lib/actions/{action-state,action-workspace,merchant-action,resolved-action-context}.server.js`, `app/lib/shopify/agentic-runtime/action-chat.server.js`, `app/components/daily-home.tsx`, `tests/{agentic-shopify-runtime,action-workspace-v2}.test.mjs`.

- **Agentic Action Chat now authorizes Shopify reads from the live installed app scopes instead of stale session metadata.** The universal gateway probes `currentAppInstallation.accessScopes` and caches that fresh result briefly per Admin client, so a stale local `Session.scope` can no longer falsely deny catalogue reads such as `products` while genuine missing Shopify scopes still block. OAuth callback paths also reconcile persisted session and connector scopes from the same live Shopify source when a token is available, so local metadata converges after reauthorization. Verified on `jefe-local-store.myshopify.com`: the exact pre-acceptance collection-scope chat ran with local `Session.scope` still missing `read_products`, admitted the `products` read through the gateway, returned `provider_result` / `OK`, materialised a concrete product scope and performed no Shopify writes. `app/lib/shopify/{api/gateway,installed-scopes}.server.js`, `app/{shopify.server,routes/auth.$}.tsx`, `tests/{shopify-api-gateway,agentic-shopify-runtime}.test.mjs`.

- **Agentic onboarding recommendations now surface without requiring a legacy insight row.** Fast onboarding now builds a recommendation-native presentation for Luna-generated recommendations, so an existing proposed Action such as the dev-store white-wine collection recommendation renders directly from its rationale, evidence and expected outcome instead of falling into the “double-checking” state when `supportingInsightIds` is empty. Legacy insight-backed recommendations still render through their `MerchantInsightFinding`. `app/lib/onboarding/fast-onboarding.server.js`, `app/components/fast-value-onboarding.tsx`, `tests/fast-onboarding.test.mjs`.

- **Agentic recommendation retry now creates a fresh Luna investigation instead of recycling the failed run.** Explicit onboarding/Plan retries now create a new `MerchantPlanRun` linked to the previous attempt by `retryOfRunId`, `baseSnapshotHash`, `onboardingEpoch` and `attemptNumber`, requeue the deduped `agentic_recommendation_generate` worker job with the new run ID, pass prior validation/tool diagnostics into Luna, and classify terminal outcomes as validation or investigation failures instead of a generic blocked state. Verified on `jefe-local-store.myshopify.com`: the failed run `b55ba853-fe79-447d-8e6f-795ed3a41b92` recovered as `a980f6ee-7485-4867-b151-dbf1d58daf78`, then a repeated immutable-snapshot retry completed as `3b2ac86c-f466-4e07-b411-96955caf193f`; the current surfaced recommendation is `695ff7a2-986c-41ff-922b-0e99349827b8` / proposed Action `043d50ea-fe15-440a-a671-7c8b14664c33`, with Shopify reads through the gateway and no Shopify writes. `app/lib/shopify/agentic-runtime/{recommendation-service,recommendation-agent}.server.js`, `tests/{agentic-shopify-runtime,fast-onboarding}.test.mjs`.

- **Agentic Actions opened from onboarding now keep their semantic recommendation context.** The `Work on this` path now materialises a stable semantic Action revision, projects Luna-generated Shopify Actions as `Proposed` / `Ready to discuss`, carries recommendation rationale, known candidate scope, constraints, evidence refs and expected Shopify effects into Action Chat, and avoids the legacy “product scope has not been discovered” generic fallback for new agentic Actions. Constraint edits on these Actions update the semantic draft revision without creating legacy Change Sets or Shopify writes. `app/lib/onboarding/fast-onboarding.server.js`, `app/lib/actions/{action-workspace,merchant-action,resolved-action-context,action-state,action-command,action-constraint}.server.js`, `app/lib/actions/agent/tool-registry.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-memory/{context-retriever,general-chat}.server.js`, `tests/{action-workspace-v2,merchant-action,action-runtime}.test.mjs`.

- **Agentic onboarding recommendations now open editable Actions instead of trying to execute from the first CTA.** The fast-onboarding CTA now says “Work on this” for Luna-generated recommendations, creates or resolves one canonical semantic `MerchantAction`, opens a focused Action Chat through the real app handoff, and leaves the Action unaccepted with no Shopify writes or legacy recommendation-review queueing. `app/lib/onboarding/fast-onboarding.server.js`, `app/routes/app._index.tsx`, `app/components/fast-value-onboarding.tsx`, `tests/fast-onboarding.test.mjs`.

- **New agentic recommendation runs now enqueue their backfill job instead of self-reusing a just-created queued run.** The queue helper now only reuses an active agentic run when a non-terminal `agentic_recommendation_generate` job already exists, which fixes the goals-to-recommendation handoff. `app/lib/shopify/agentic-runtime/recommendation-service.server.js`, `tests/merchant-goals.test.mjs`.

## 2026-08-20

### Added

- **Jefe now discovers Shopify actions as versioned capabilities before turning store evidence into recommendations.** The new Shopify capability catalogue separates Admin GraphQL technical facts from semantic effects, qualification evidence and execution admission; Merchant Plan now searches that catalogue from store conditions, qualifies candidates before Luna sees them, rejects inventory transfers when no source stock exists, and preserves the existing typed adapters only as executor bindings. A new golden-path runner validates capability discovery, dynamic recommendation qualification, scope expansion, workflow ownership, live Luna focused-action journeys and the safe execution wiring. `app/lib/shopify/capabilities/**`, `app/lib/merchant-plan/candidates.server.js`, `app/lib/actions/{action-intent,action-resolution,shopify-action-capabilities}.server.js`, `scripts/{shopify-capabilities-discover,eval-jefe-golden-path}.mjs`, `tests/{shopify-capability-catalog,merchant-plan}.test.mjs`, `docs/ops/shopify-capability-catalog.md`, `GLOSSARY.md`.

- **The dynamic-capability golden path now has an explicit real development Shopify write gate.** `--real-shopify` no longer depends on synthetic seeding env names and no longer records a placeholder skip: it runs a dedicated dev-store proof behind `JEFE_GOLDEN_PATH_SHOPIFY_WRITE_ENABLED`, shop allowlisting and an offline-session-or-explicit-token credential source. The proof creates a normal ActionExecution, dispatches through the existing approve→execute inventory-transfer wire, persists the Shopify transfer receipt, reloads it and retries idempotently. Candidate discovery also now shapes opportunities from provider manifests and resolver summaries instead of old `execute:*:*` business refs; the remaining legacy refs are documented as executor/workflow compatibility. `scripts/{eval-jefe-golden-path,eval-real-dev-shopify-golden-path}.mjs`, `app/lib/merchant-plan/candidates.server.js`, `tests/shopify-capability-catalog.test.mjs`, `docs/ops/{shopify-capability-catalog,dynamic-capability-legacy-audit-2026-08-20}.md`, `.env.example`.

- **Accepted replenishment recommendations now project as an Action Workspace instead of a rigid checklist.** Jefe keeps the workflow rows for compatibility, but accepted restock work now carries a V2 workspace projection with decision artifacts, merchant actions, external waits, current focus and semantic labels. New restock recommendations no longer materialise “Review low-cover inventory” as merchant-facing future work when the evidence already identified the products; that diagnosis stays in the recommendation rationale. The home surface now reads the workspace current focus, focused chat state includes the workspace projection, and the live LLM evaluator rejects reintroduced inventory-review steps while reporting workspace state. `app/lib/actions/action-workspace.server.js`, `app/lib/actions/{merchant-action,action-state,action-replanner}.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-plan/{prompt,service}.server.js`, `app/components/daily-home.tsx`, `scripts/eval-focused-action-live.mjs`, `tests/action-workspace-v2.test.mjs`, `GLOSSARY.md`.

- **Restock actions now have first-class current scope, canonical proposals and Shopify capability truth.** Focused action chat can add a Shopify product outside the original recommendation evidence, resolve it against the local Shopify mirror, preserve that scope across replans, and immediately rebuild the canonical replenishment proposal. Supplier emails now carry proposal provenance so old quantities cannot be relabelled with a new cover target. The new Shopify action capability catalog separates intended actor from execution availability; Shopify purchase-order creation is represented as a Jefe-owned integration limitation because the public API does not expose a supplier PO create mutation. `app/lib/actions/{action-scope,action-capability,action-state,resolved-action-context,shopify-action-capabilities}.server.js`, `app/lib/actions/agent/{agent-loop,tool-registry}.server.js`, `app/lib/actions/assist-steps/handlers/{replenishment-proposal,supplier-email-draft}.server.js`, `scripts/eval-focused-action-live.mjs`, `tests/action-scope-v2.test.mjs`, `docs/ops/{action-workspace-v2-design-2026-08-20,shopify-action-capabilities}.md`, `GLOSSARY.md`.

### Changed

- **Plan recommendations now have to come from grounded executable opportunities before Jefe can surface them.** Merchant Plan snapshots now prepare deterministic opportunity candidates with evidence, affected entities, initial proposal/state and enabled Shopify write capabilities before Luna prioritises the recommendation. The prompt rejects advice-only opportunities, structured validation refuses recommendation workflows without a valid `execute:*:*` capability tied to the selected opportunity, and generation dry-runs the selected execute steps against the same primitive resolvers used for proposal creation. The selected opportunity is copied into Plan run results and Merchant Action progress so action state starts from the evidence and proposal Jefe already had. `app/lib/actions/{action-resolution,merchant-action}.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-plan/{candidates,constants,prompt,schema,service,step-capabilities}.server.js`, `tests/merchant-plan.test.mjs`, `GLOSSARY.md`.

- **Focused Action prompts now follow the Recommendation-to-Execution contract.** Action Chat builds its system prompt from the current action's callable tool catalogue, so Luna is no longer instructed to use tools that are unavailable for that action. The semantic replanner now prompts for a complete desired workspace, can return `UNPROGRESSABLE` instead of converting missing Jefe execution into merchant work, and supplier-email artifacts are versioned and generated only from the current canonical proposal. `app/lib/actions/agent/agent-loop.server.js`, `app/lib/actions/action-replanner.server.js`, `app/lib/actions/assist-steps/{generate-artifact,handlers/supplier-email-draft}.server.js`, `tests/focused-action-prompt-contracts.test.mjs`.

- **Jefe text LLM calls now default to OpenAI GPT-5.6 Luna with cached-token cost accounting.** The app has a first-class OpenAI provider, Luna pricing, prompt-cache token capture, local env/docs defaults, OpenAI vision fallback for scanned attachments, and a live focused-action evaluator that reports call count, cached input, latency and projected cost. The first-party OpenAI adapter now omits unsupported sampling overrides for Luna while keeping them for OpenAI-compatible providers, and provider HTTP failures record sanitized upstream codes/messages. Focused action chat now skips synchronous passive-memory extraction, trims reply/extraction output caps, and rejects duplicate successful tool calls inside a turn so merchants get the work result instead of a leaked loop guard. `app/lib/llm/{config,provider,pricing,usage-recorder,errors}.server.js`, `app/lib/llm/providers/{openai,openai-compatible}.server.js`, `app/lib/{merchant-memory/general-chat,merchant-memory/passive-memory,attachments/read-attachment}.server.js`, `app/lib/actions/agent/{agent-loop,turn-outcome}.server.js`, `scripts/eval-focused-action-live.mjs`, `prisma/schema.prisma`.

### Fixed

- **Inventory-transfer approval no longer appears as merchant purchase-order work.** Luna's Plan contract now rejects inventory-transfer workflows that convert approval into `merchant_action:external_purchase_order` or introduce dependency cycles, and the Action Workspace read path sanitises existing transfer workflows so the visible plan is merchant approval followed by Jefe-owned Shopify inventory-transfer execution. Approval-required execute steps now preserve `intendedActor: JEFE`, carry `approvalRequired: true`, render with an approval CTA instead of “Mark done”, and complete from the Shopify execution receipt after approval. `app/lib/merchant-plan/{prompt,schema}.server.js`, `app/lib/actions/action-workspace.server.js`, `app/components/daily-home.tsx`, `tests/{merchant-plan,action-workspace-v2}.test.mjs`.

- **Shopify inventory-transfer recommendations now prove the transfer can execute before Luna sees them.** The restock resolver narrows low-cover opportunities to tracked inventory items, the approval wire now builds the real Shopify GraphQL client from the merchant's offline token, and applied transfer receipts store the returned Shopify inventory transfer id in the current action ledger. `app/lib/actions/{action-resolution,inventory-transfer-adapter,inventory-transfer-shopify-client,wire-inventory-transfer-execution}.server.js`, `tests/{canonical-action-state,execute-approved-action}.test.mjs`.

- **Fast onboarding now stops waiting when Luna finds no grounded executable first move.** If the first Merchant Plan run completes as insufficient data after the grounded-opportunity gate, the Context screen now shows a terminal, merchant-readable message instead of continuing to say the page will move on by itself. `app/lib/onboarding/fast-onboarding.server.js`, `tests/onboarding-flow.test.mjs`.

- **Fast onboarding no longer gets stuck after an unsupported bootstrap recommendation is reconciled away.** When bootstrap briefly creates a recommendation that full memory later supersedes, and full learning has already finished with no surfaceable first action, the Context screen now exits the waiting state and shows the same grounded-action fallback instead of polling forever. `app/lib/onboarding/fast-onboarding.server.js`, `tests/fast-onboarding.test.mjs`.

- **No-recommendation traces now explain why a restock transfer was rejected.** The recommendation dry-run now records a safe reason such as `no_source_stock_for_low_cover_items` when low-cover evidence exists but Shopify has no stock to transfer, so onboarding diagnostics can distinguish genuinely inapplicable actions from missing evidence or read failures. `app/lib/actions/action-resolution.server.js`, `tests/merchant-plan.test.mjs`.

- **Fast onboarding no longer waits forever after a Luna first-plan timeout.** Merchant Plan generation now has a feature-appropriate 60s hard timeout instead of the interactive 15s replanner bound, while the Context screen treats failed or disabled first-plan runs as retryable terminal states. Pressing Try again requeues the Merchant Plan from the durable evidence instead of restarting the Shopify/bootstrap read. `app/lib/merchant-plan/service.server.js`, `app/lib/onboarding/fast-onboarding.server.js`, `app/components/fast-value-onboarding.tsx`.

- **The Luna action replanner can no longer hang invisibly or drift canonical workflow wording.** LLM usage now records a started row before every provider inference and updates it to ok, error or timed_out, the OpenAI adapter bounds both request and response-body parsing, 429 responses no longer wait on a stalled error body, and the action replanner has a 15s hard timeout with phase logs. Focused action turns now stop after a successful mutating batch instead of re-asking Luna to repeat the same mutation, and capability-backed replans canonicalise Shopify inventory transfer, purchase-order and supplier lead-time step labels. `app/lib/llm/{provider,usage-recorder}.server.js`, `app/lib/llm/providers/openai-compatible.server.js`, `app/lib/actions/{action-replanner}.server.js`, `app/lib/actions/agent/agent-loop.server.js`, `tests/{openai-compatible-provider,llm-cost-ledger,focused-action-semantic-evaluations}.test.mjs`.

- **Focused action chat now rebuilds the workflow when a merchant explains how the work is really done.** A merchant can say “We use Shopify transfers for this” or “We call the supplier instead of emailing” and Jefe now sends that semantic instruction through a dedicated action replanner. The replanner generates step titles, dependencies and capabilities itself, preserves current plan decisions such as 90-day cover, and hides low-level tool validation like `add_plan_step needs "title"` from the merchant. Added a dev-only live LLM evaluator for these focused-action journeys. `app/lib/actions/{action-replanner,action-command,action-state,merchant-action}.server.js`, `app/lib/actions/agent/{agent-loop,tool-registry}.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-plan/step-capabilities.server.js`, `GLOSSARY.md`, `scripts/eval-focused-action-live.mjs`, `tests/focused-action-semantic-evaluations.test.mjs`.

- **Semantic replanning now replaces invalid supplier-email workflow instead of appending purchase orders to it.** Corrections such as “we don't email suppliers; we create a PO directly” remove the supplier-communication step from the current desired plan, keep the replenishment proposal and current cover/scope decisions, and stamp the purchase-order item as an integration limitation rather than `MERCHANT · needs you`. ## 2026-08-19

### Fixed

- **Changing a replenishment plan's cover days now rebuilds all three workflow steps consistently — inventory review, proposal, and supplier email always derive from one canonical revision.** The root cause was that `persistRecommendationWorkflow` was creating steps without mapping the LLM's placeholder IDs (`step_1`, `step_2`) to real Postgres UUIDs, so `dependsOnStepIds` contained strings that never matched anything in the dependency graph. All downstream deps were treated as satisfied regardless of state. Fixed with a UUID mapping table at creation time and a `orderIndex`-based fallback in `resolveWorkChain` for legacy actions. Also fixed `invalidateDownstreamFromCurrentFocus` to handle the all-completed case (a plan edit after a full run now correctly marks stale steps as `needs_updating`). Added `ADD_PLAN_STEP` command and `add_plan_step` tool so merchants can extend a replenishment workflow through conversation ("add a step to create the Shopify transfer"). Introduced the typed `shopify.inventoryTransfer.create` adapter — flag-gated behind `INVENTORY_TRANSFER_EXECUTE_ENABLED`, with preview, idempotency, and a blast-radius cap of 50 items. `write_inventory_transfers` OAuth scope added. 16 regression tests and 5 focused-action semantic eval scenarios added. `app/lib/actions/action-command.server.js`, `app/lib/actions/agent/assist-runner.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-plan/service.server.js`, `app/lib/actions/inventory-transfer-adapter.server.js`.

- **Completed workflow steps now immediately promote their dependants — no stale "waiting" after step 1 finishes.** `startActionStep` previously only reconciled the workflow when there was no active step at all; if a prior `completeActionStepRun` left a dependent step in `waiting` (e.g. race, legacy state), a follow-up `START_STEP` would fail with a confusing reason. The guard now runs `reconcileWorkflow` before evaluating startability whenever the current step is not yet startable, so a "completed prerequisite → waiting dependent" state is always corrected before the command is validated. `app/lib/actions/action-step-lifecycle.server.js`.

- **`START_STEP` failure messages now say what actually blocked the step.** The single fallback reply "I couldn't start that step just now." has been replaced with structured messages for each failure reason: already running, needs attention (with step title and detail), needs rebuilding after a plan change, waiting on a named prerequisite, no active step, wrong step targeted, or claim race. `app/lib/actions/action-command.server.js`.

- **Focused factual questions about the action now answer from plan state, not a generic workflow recap.** A question like "what replenishment window is that?" or "how many days cover?" was falling through to `buildPlanRecapReply` because `answerFocusedAction` only handled `/why/` messages. The function now reads the resolved action context and, for cover-period questions, answers directly from `plan.values.coverDays`; for markdown questions from `plan.values.markdownPercent`; for why-questions with a grounded breakdown. Store-wide questions still route to Merchant Memory. `app/lib/actions/action-command.server.js`.

Clarification stays stateful, hypothetical language does not mutate, buttons still call exact commands, and if the interpreter is down Jefe refuses unsafe natural-language mutations rather than guessing from regex. `app/lib/actions/{action-interpreter,action-command,plan-chat,action-workflow-navigation}.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-memory/general-chat.server.js`, `tests/{action-interpreter,focused-action-golden-path,workflow-navigation}.test.mjs`.

Navigation runs through structured commands (`ADVANCE_STEP`, `GO_BACK`, `GO_TO_STEP`, `SKIP_STEP`) with dependency-aware downstream invalidation when plan or scope changes make later steps stale. Ambiguous targets such as “Go back to the quantities” ask a targeted clarification question that persists across the next turn. `app/lib/actions/{action-workflow-navigation,action-command,action-step-lifecycle,plan-chat}.server.js`, `tests/workflow-navigation.test.mjs`.

### Fixed

Accepting now runs the same workflow unlock as the action plan (`ready` / `needs_merchant` for the first eligible step, `waiting` for the rest), leftover pending rows are healed on action load, and “move to the next step” on an unstarted step advises the merchant to start it rather than skipping or dumping a recap. `app/lib/actions/{action-step-lifecycle,action-workflow-navigation,merchant-action,plan-chat,action-interpreter}.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-plan/service.server.js`, `app/lib/onboarding/{fast-onboarding,bootstrap}.server.js`, `app/components/daily-home.tsx`, `tests/{action-step-lifecycle,workflow-navigation,fast-onboarding,merchant-plan}.test.mjs`.

- **Focused action chat now answers from the current action instead of generic store commentary.** Questions like “Show me what you're proposing right now” were classified as plain `ANSWER` and skipped Action Runtime, so Merchant Memory replied with unrelated product counts. Focused conversations now route through Action Runtime first (with `INSPECT_PROPOSAL`, scope, status, and constraint read handlers), and only explicit store-wide questions such as “How many products do I sell overall?” still use generic Merchant Memory. `app/lib/actions/action-command.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-memory/general-chat.server.js`, `tests/action-runtime.test.mjs`.

- **Natural-language scope and plan changes now persist through the replenishment golden path.** Phrases like “Only replenish Pear Skin Sipon”, “Don't include Picnic Xinomavro”, and “Actually use 60 days” map to structured scope/plan commands, rebuild proposals from resolved action state, and propagate into assist step artifacts. - **Focused-chat plan revisions and exclusions now survive into execution.** Restock assist was still ordering Picnic Xinomavro at 120-day cover after the merchant had set 90 days and excluded that product, because step handlers reconstructed work from defaults instead of the current action. Chat, Change Sets, assist artifacts and step runs now share one resolved action context, persist the input they used, and cannot reintroduce an excluded product. Assist previews show recommended quantities rather than asking the merchant to accept before they can see the proposal. `app/lib/actions/{resolved-action-context,action-changeset,action-command,action-constraint,action-step-lifecycle}.server.js`, `app/lib/actions/assist-steps/**`, `prisma/migrations/20260818193500_action_runtime_resolved_context/migration.sql`, `tests/action-runtime.test.mjs`.

- **Chat now uses Gemini when Groq cannot take the prompt, instead of skipping the model and returning a canned fallback.** Local chat was sending an 8,000-token request cap that Groq rejected as a hard input-limit error, so the configured Gemini fallback never ran. ## 2026-08-13

### Added

- **Jefe can now draw what it is telling you — ask where your orders come from and the answer arrives with the chart, inside the reply.** Some answers are bad sentences and good pictures: a channel breakdown, a price spread, revenue moving over 90 days. Jefe now adds a chart when the shape of the answer calls for one, and deliberately does not for a single number or a yes/no. ⛔ **A chart may not contain a number the analysis does not** — every value is checked against the computed packet before it is drawn, and a chart carrying anything else is discarded while the reply still sends. That guard is the whole point: an honest paragraph beside a flattering picture is worse than no picture, because a chart reads as computed fact and nobody cross-checks the axes against the words. Display rounding is allowed; invention is not. The words also stay the answer — the model is told never to write "as shown below", so a reader whose client strips the image, or who uses a screen reader, loses nothing (screen readers get the actual numbers, not the word "chart"). Drawn as inline SVG with no charting library and no client script, so it appears in the first paint and cannot fail separately from the message it belongs to. A chart of no data is refused outright. `app/lib/charts/chart-layout.js`, `app/lib/merchant-memory/{commerce-analyst,general-chat}.server.js`, `app/components/daily-home.tsx`, `tests/chart-in-chat.test.mjs`.

- **Merchants can now point Jefe back at a file they already sent — "use the cost sheet I gave you" — instead of uploading it again.** Keeping a file without being able to draw on it again is only a file that hasn't been deleted, so the chat composer now offers your kept files alongside the paperclip; picking one puts it into the conversation in exactly the shape a fresh upload takes, so Jefe cannot tell a sheet sent last month from one sent a second ago. Using a file records when it was last used, which is what keeps "the invoice I sent you in April" findable. Files can also be downloaded back out of the library. ⛔ The download resolves ownership from the **session**, never from the URL: a file id belonging to another store returns nothing rather than someone else's invoice, and files are served as an attachment rather than inline, because a merchant-supplied file rendered on our own origin is a stored-XSS primitive. A file deleted since it was picked says so instead of quietly sending a bare message. `app/routes/app.library.$fileId.download.tsx`, `app/routes/app._index.tsx`, `app/components/{daily-home,merchant-library-view}.tsx`, `app/lib/attachments/merchant-file.server.js`, `tests/merchant-library.test.mjs`.

- **Merchants can now keep a file in Jefe rather than have it read once and forgotten — a cost sheet, a supplier invoice, a stock count, all in one place under Your library.** Every upload asks the question: keep this, or is it one-off analysis? **Default is not to keep** — storing by default would turn every casual screenshot into a retained record nobody chose to create. The library shows **what Jefe read out of each file**, not just its name: "invoice-april.pdf" says nothing about whether Jefe understood it, and the first lines of the extract let a merchant delete a misread file rather than meet it later inside bad advice. Removing a file is a real delete, not a flag — a merchant who asks Jefe to forget a document means gone. ⭐ The bytes live in Postgres deliberately rather than in object storage: deletion becomes a DELETE, which makes erasure trivially correct and verifiable, backups come with the database, and no vendor, region or retention policy had to be decided first; moving to S3 later is a migration of one column, not a rewrite. Every listing and every prompt reads the extract and never the bytes, which exist only so a merchant can download their own file back. ⛔ The copy deliberately never says "private": keeping and discarding BOTH send the file to a model to be read, and the choice is only about what happens afterwards — a merchant who reads "don't save" as "stays private" has been misled at the moment of transmission, not when we notice. ⚠️ Kept text is not PII-scrubbed (removed across all surfaces the same day), so an invoice held here keeps its customer names and emails at rest until deleted. `prisma/migrations/20260813171010_merchant_files/migration.sql`, `app/lib/attachments/merchant-file.server.js`, `app/components/merchant-library-view.tsx`, `app/routes/{app._index,app.settings}.tsx`, `tests/merchant-library.test.mjs`.

- **Jefe now reads Excel workbooks, Word documents and PDFs exactly — the words and numbers come out as they were written, not as a model described them.** A spreadsheet's cells are read straight through: a formula gives its **result** rather than its source ("=B2*3" is not a margin), a comma inside a cell cannot silently become an extra column, and a multi-sheet workbook says which tab each block came from. PDFs with a text layer — most invoices and statements — are extracted rather than looked at, which is faster, free, and cannot invent a total; a **scanned** PDF has no text to extract, so that and only that falls back to the vision model, because telling a merchant their invoice was blank when it plainly is not would be the worst available answer. ⛔ Legacy binary `.xls` is deliberately excluded: the only npm library that reads it is frozen at a version with an unfixed prototype-pollution advisory, so a merchant with a true `.xls` is asked to re-save it — ten seconds of their time against a permanent dependency. A corrupt or password-protected file names a way forward instead of failing. `app/lib/attachments/extract-document-text.server.js`, `app/lib/attachments/{attachment-limits.js,read-attachment.server.js}`, `tests/document-extraction.test.mjs`.

- **A merchant can now send Jefe a CSV — a cost export, a supplier price list, a stock count — and every row is read exactly rather than described.** Text files never touch a vision model: decoding is instant, free, and a model cannot hallucinate a cost that is sitting there in row 40, so the numbers survive character-for-character. This matters more than it sounds — **cost-per-item is the input Jefe's margin and clearance work is blocked without**, and a merchant can now simply send it. A file longer than Jefe can carry is truncated on a whole-line boundary and **says so in its own text** ("showing the first 300 of 4,000 lines"), because the alternative is Jefe confidently naming your worst margin having silently read a tenth of the file. Windows reports a plain `.csv` as an Excel MIME type, so the filename is consulted too — deciding on the MIME type alone would have told a merchant to save their CSV as the thing it already was. Excel and video are refused **with a way forward** rather than a dead end: "save it as CSV and I'll read every row", and "a photo of the same thing works". A spreadsheet renamed to `.csv` is caught and refused rather than described as data. `app/lib/attachments/{attachment-limits.js,read-attachment.server.js}`, `tests/attachment-composer.test.mjs`.

- **Changelog posts now carry the commit they came from, and stop arriving twice.** The app's changelog watcher and an agent were both announcing the same entry to #jefe-slack. Hand-posting is now retired in favour of the watcher, which has a property no discipline can match: it reads the `CHANGELOG.md` inside its own deploy, so it physically cannot announce something before that something is running in production. Its posts now end with the running deploy's short SHA and a separator, so an entry can be traced back to the diff that caused it. The words are still written by hand — only the transport is automated. `app/services/changelog/changelog-watcher.server.js`, `CLAUDE.md`.

- **A merchant can now send Jefe a photo or a PDF from the chat composer.** Jefe could already read a file, but nothing in the app let anyone give it one — the whole path stopped one input short of the merchant. There is now a paperclip in the composer: pick a photo of a shelf or a supplier invoice, send it with a note or on its own, and Jefe reads it and answers. **Derive and discard** (following the 2026-07-31 voice-note ruling): the bytes go to the model, the words come back, the file is never stored — so no blob vendor, no retention policy and no schema change. What Jefe read is written into the thread **labelled as a reading of the file**, not as the merchant's own words, which is their only chance to catch a misread invoice before Jefe reasons on it. The allow-list (photos + PDF) and the 8MB cap live in one browser-safe module so the composer refuses a bad file instantly and the server refuses the same file again on arrival; an oversized body is rejected from `Content-Length` before it is buffered. A file Jefe cannot read produces a sentence in the composer rather than silence. ⛔ Extracted text is **not** PII-scrubbed (see the security entry below), so an invoice's customer names, emails and phone numbers reach the thread verbatim. `app/lib/attachments/{attachment-limits.js,attachment-message.server.js,read-attachment.server.js}`, `app/routes/app._index.tsx`, `app/components/daily-home.tsx`, `tests/attachment-composer.test.mjs`.

- **The four things Jefe learned about your store today now read as sentences instead of raw data.** They were being worked out correctly and shown to you as a blob of fields, which is not something anyone can agree or disagree with — and being able to disagree is most of the point. Jefe now says which offer is doing your discounting (and whether customers are choosing it or it's just applying itself), how your customers split between one-off buyers and the small group carrying your revenue, where people first found you, and — once acquisition tracking is on — which of those sources brings customers who come back. **Each one refuses to overstate.** Where Jefe couldn't work out your customers' normal buying rhythm it says nothing about who's gone quiet, rather than implying nobody has. Channel comparisons carry no percentage at all, because those figures are still early and a merchant reading one as "my repeat rate" would be badly misled — so it compares two sources and says to treat it as a comparison. And two channels a few points apart produce silence rather than a finding. `app/lib/merchant-memory/belief-statement.server.js`, `tests/belief-statement-new-beliefs.test.mjs`.

- **Jefe now gives every action its own durable identity, separate from the chat where it is discussed.** A recommendation still says why Jefe thinks work is worth doing, and an action execution still records the approval/write/outcome ledger, but there is now a first-class Merchant Action between them that represents the merchant-facing piece of work over time. Chats can be blank, or focused on exactly one Merchant Action; other actions can be referenced in the thread as read-only context without becoming the thing Jefe is allowed to update by default. Existing `action:*` conversations and recommendation/execution rows are backfilled into the new structure, and the home now opens on "Your next move", Chats, In Progress Actions and Action history with a persistent connected "Working on" panel inside focused chats. Focused chats open at the latest message, keep the composer pinned while only the transcript scrolls, switch-action confirmation closes as soon as the merchant submits it, and the working-on card now uses a compact two-line action title. The "Talk this through" chooser now matches the chat reuse flow: explanatory copy, real existing-chat counts, a bordered chat card and clean Start/Cancel actions. `prisma/schema.prisma`, `prisma/migrations/20260813173000_focused_actions/migration.sql`, `app/lib/actions/merchant-action.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-memory/focused-action-chat.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-memory/{general-chat,context-retriever}.server.js`, `app/routes/app._index.tsx`, `app/components/daily-home.tsx`, `tests/{focused-actions-migration,merchant-action,focused-action-chat,merchant-context-retriever,memory-surface-wiring,home-nav-guard,action-resolution}.test.mjs`.

- **Recommendations now carry a route to completion, not just a one-line move.** The Plan model now asks the LLM for a workflow of concrete steps, validates each step against what Jefe can honestly do, help with, ask evidence for, or leave to the merchant, and persists those steps as first-class records beside the recommendation. Draft recommendations show the steps before acceptance; accepting a recommendation activates the workflow and only then emits executable step proposals through the existing typed action ledger. This is a deliberate reset cutover: recommendation-level `executionSteps` and `actionIntent`, plus the direct ActionExecution → Recommendation link, are removed instead of backfilled, so existing environments need fresh recommendation generation after deploy. Stale action execution is safer too: approval now refuses non-executable statuses centrally and every wire claims `proposed → approved` with compare-and-set before it writes. `prisma/schema.prisma`, `prisma/migrations/20260813190000_workflow_cutover_drop_legacy/migration.sql`, `app/lib/merchant-plan/{prompt,schema,service,step-capabilities}.server.js`, `app/lib/actions/{action-resolution,execute-approved-action,wire-*-execution}.server.js`, `app/components/daily-home.tsx`, `app/routes/app._index.tsx`, `tests/merchant-plan.test.mjs`.

- **Jefe can now tell which marketing brings customers who come back — not just customers. Built, tested, and silent until attribution is switched on.** Knowing where orders come from and knowing who returns were two separate things Jefe could see, and neither answered the question that actually decides a budget: is the channel sending the most people sending the ones worth having? It now groups customers by where they first arrived from and compares those groups on how many came back and what they went on to spend, so a channel delivering twice the volume and nobody who returns stops looking like the winner. **Deliberately refuses to give you a repeat rate.** Where-from is only recorded for customers who arrived after the feature is enabled, so they're all recent and most simply haven't had time to reorder yet — the numbers are floors, valid for comparing channels against each other and badly wrong read any other way, and the answer says so in its own value rather than in a footnote. Customers who arrived in the last month are left out entirely, so whichever channel is busiest right now isn't punished for it, and Jefe stays quiet unless two channels have enough established customers to compare, because a comparison needs two sides. `app/lib/merchant-memory/shopify-derivations.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-memory/deterministic-belief-registry.server.js`, `tests/channel-quality.test.mjs`.

### Fixed

- **Accepting a first-run recommendation now activates its workflow, not just the label on the action.** The normal Plan accept path already flipped workflow steps from draft to pending, but the fast-onboarding approval path only marked the recommendation accepted. The result was a focused chat saying "Accepted" while the proposed steps were still draft behind the scenes. Both tracked and executed onboarding accepts now activate the workflow and pending steps in the same transaction, and the local trial row was repaired. `app/lib/onboarding/fast-onboarding.server.js`, `tests/fast-onboarding.test.mjs`.

- **Focused action chats now understand "this" and show the workflow without opening the card.** The focused chat context now carries the recommendation workflow steps as `proposedSteps`, the model gets the current action as explicit context before generic Merchant Memory, and the working-on card renders the first workflow steps directly under the accepted action title. `app/lib/merchant-memory/{context-retriever,general-chat}.server.js`, `app/components/daily-home.tsx`, `tests/{general-chat-fallback-prose,merchant-context-retriever}.test.mjs`.

- **Workflow steps now use the merchant-facing step layout across the next-move card and focused chat.** The card header shows status plus step count, expanded workflow rows use numbered spacing with Jefe/Merchant owner pills, mode labels such as "Jefe can help" or "Waiting for evidence", descriptions and right-aligned step status, and the action serializer now preserves step metadata instead of reducing workflows to labels. `app/components/daily-home.tsx`, `app/lib/actions/merchant-action.server.js`.

- **Focused action chat now sends the current action as an explicit model input with operational context.** The LLM prompt now includes `currentAction` beside the full Merchant Context packet, with workflow steps, low-cover product evidence, default cover assumptions and the restock quantity formula packaged as model-readable primitives. Code prepares facts and formulas; the model decides whether the merchant is asking for a supplier draft, a redraft, a quantity change or another action-specific response. `app/lib/merchant-memory/general-chat.server.js`, `tests/general-chat-fallback-prose.test.mjs`.

- **Workflow step chat can now update step status through a validated structured model output.** The general chat reply schema accepts `workflowStepUpdates`, but server code only applies allowed statuses (`pending`, `in_progress`, `blocked`, `completed`, `skipped`) to step IDs that belong to the current focused action, and logs applied updates. The workflow row layout now removes the left Jefe/Merchant owner pills, keeps mode labels such as "Jefe can help" / "Needs merchant action", and restores right-aligned step status. `app/lib/merchant-memory/general-chat.server.js`, `app/components/daily-home.tsx`, `app/lib/actions/merchant-action.server.js`, `tests/general-chat-fallback-prose.test.mjs`.

- **⛔ SECURITY: PII scrubbing removed across every surface (founder's decision, 2026-08-13).** Customer emails, phone numbers, payment identifiers and names typed into chat, extracted from an uploaded file, or captured in logs now pass through **unmasked** — into LLM provider prompts, stored conversation threads, Merchant Memory, Sentry, and the activity event log that feeds `admin.mynamejefe.com`. That panel is cross-merchant and currently has no login (also a founder decision, revisit ~early September); the two compound, and this entry exists so that is on the record rather than discovered. **Deliberately NOT removed:** credential masking — API keys, bearer tokens, `shpat_` Shopify access tokens, passwords, and SSN/card/CVV keys. A leaked access token is account takeover rather than a privacy question, and that was not what was asked for. To restore: the removed patterns are in git history at this commit, in `sanitizeMemoryText` and `redact`; the ~9 inverted test assertions marked `PII scrubbing REMOVED 2026-08-13` are the full list of what to flip back. `app/lib/observability/redact.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-memory/episodic-memory.server.js`, +7 test files.

  **Completing the sweep (same day, follow-up commit).** The first pass missed the tool layer: every result Jefe's Shopify intelligence tools returned to the model was still dropping customer-shaped keys and rewriting emails and phone numbers to `[redacted-*]`. That is now credential-masking only, matching the rest. Docs that asserted the old guarantee were corrected rather than left to mislead the next agent — `AGENTS.md` now says explicitly that **there is no safety net under the "never log customer PII" rule** and that its absence is deliberate, so nobody re-adds scrubbing on sight. ⛔ **`docs/growth/privacy-policy-draft.md` is now marked as blocking publication**: it promises technical logs carry "not customer PII" and Sentry events are redacted, and neither is true — a privacy policy is enforceable in a way an internal doc is not, so it needs the founder's call before it ships. **One scrubber deliberately left in place, needing a decision:** `customerIdentity` stores only a sha256 `emailHash` and a masked display email — the plaintext customer email is *never persisted*. Removing that is a schema change, and irreversible in one direction: rows written under the current model can never be un-masked, because the plaintext was never kept. `app/lib/shopify/intelligence-tools.server.js`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `context/07_architecture.md`, `docs/growth/privacy-policy-draft.md`.

- **The half of Jefe's customer read that spots people going quiet was dead on arrival — today, hours after it shipped.** It could tell you how your customers split between one-off, returning and loyal, but the other half — who has gone quiet, how long your customers normally leave between orders, and what that lapsed spend is worth — always came back as "not enough repeat customers to tell", for every store, no matter how many repeat customers they had. Jefe was reading two dates it had never actually loaded from the database, so they were simply blank. Nothing errored and every test passed, because the tests handed it complete customer records while the real thing fetched three fields. Now it loads what it reads, and a test asserts that against the real query rather than against a convenient fake, so the next belief that quietly depends on an unloaded field fails loudly instead of reporting "can't tell" forever. `app/lib/merchant-memory/shopify-derivations.server.js`, `tests/customer-cohort-mix.test.mjs`.

### Added

- **Fast onboarding now reads complete order lines and the active catalogue before making its first call.** The first read still stays bounded to a recent order window, but Jefe now paginates every line item on each selected order instead of accepting Shopify's first page as the whole basket. It also reads the full active catalogue — active products, all their variants, and all inventory levels — before choosing the first insight, so products with no recent sales can still shape the opening recommendation. Draft and archived products stay in the background backfill, and dead-stock/no-sales conclusions still wait for full-history evidence. `app/lib/onboarding/bootstrap.server.js`, `app/lib/shopify/queries.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-memory/constants.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-memory/shopify-derivations.server.js`, `tests/fast-onboarding.test.mjs`.

- **Jefe now finds what it knows using the words merchants actually use.** Jefe holds 143 things it knows about a store but can only put 40 of them in front of the model for any one question, and it was choosing which 40 by matching the merchant's words against the *internal name* of each fact. Two failures fell out of that. Anything named with a word merchants don't say was simply never found — ask "where are my orders coming from?" and the fact about exactly that was filed under "acquisition", so it sat there, correct and unread. And words that appear in dozens of internal names, like "orders", pulled in a quarter of everything Jefe knows at once, which is closer to picking at random than to choosing. Now a match counts for more when the word is distinctive and less when it's everywhere, and the facts most likely to be missed carry the phrases merchants really type — "come back", "ad spend", "promo", "churn", "instagram". The fact a merchant is already discussing still outranks everything, so follow-up questions don't lose their subject. `app/lib/merchant-memory/conversation.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-memory/deterministic-belief-registry.server.js`, `tests/belief-retrieval-vocabulary.test.mjs`.

- **Jefe now has a plain-English glossary beside the changelog.** `apps/shopify/GLOSSARY.md` explains the product, memory, chat, action and operations entities operators keep asking about: Merchant Memory, Store updates, typed adapters, action executions, Health vs Ready and the rest of the core vocabulary. The agent instructions now require future glossary additions to ship with the entity they describe and be posted to #jefe-slack once live and verified, using the same short-SHA footer discipline as changelog posts; the old context glossary points at the Markdown file so the repo does not grow competing terminology lists. `GLOSSARY.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `context/99_glossary.md`.

- **Jefe now has explicit model routing for chat vs memory-grade work.** Local setup now uses Gemini 3.5 Flash-Lite for onboarding, Merchant Memory, insights, goals and planning, with Gemini 3.1 Flash-Lite as fallback; chat uses Groq GPT-OSS 120B first and Gemini 3.5 Flash-Lite as the cross-provider fallback. The provider layer also records conservative provider input caps, so an oversized prompt skips Groq before any HTTP request instead of waiting for a 413. `/health` now shows the chat model separately from the default memory model. `app/lib/llm/config.server.js`, `app/lib/llm/provider.server.js`, `app/lib/llm/providers/*.server.js`, `app/services/deployment-health.server.js`, `tests/llm-provider-fallback.test.mjs`, `tests/health-diagnostics.test.mjs`, `apps/shopify/.env.example`.

- **The local Shopify env templates now name every action go-live flag.** `apps/shopify/.env.example` now includes the clearance, tidy-up, listing-copy and order-attribution switches instead of leaving new workspaces to infer them from code comments or a deployed Railway setting. The example keeps every path dark by default; local `.env` files can opt in deliberately. `apps/shopify/.env.example`.

- **First-run onboarding no longer dead-ends on a temporary missing recommendation.** If the bootstrap read has saved the merchant's priority but the recommendation row has not surfaced yet, Jefe now keeps checking quietly instead of showing "I don't have a first recommendation strong enough to act on." That state was a race: a manual refresh could reveal the insight a moment later, so the merchant should never be told the evidence failed. The Shopify env example also now matches the 30s LLM timeout default, so local Groq/Gemini fallback testing does not inherit the old 8s timeout. `app/lib/onboarding/fast-onboarding.server.js`, `tests/fast-onboarding.test.mjs`, `apps/shopify/.env.example`.

- **Jefe now measures Shopify intelligence coverage by the evidence it actually needs, not by how much of Shopify's API it mirrors.** The new coverage catalog asks whether Jefe can access the evidence behind its current understanding, recommendations, investigations and outcome checks, then reports the useful number: P0 coverage is **96.8%** across 31 required Shopify evidence points, with 23 covered by the canonical mirror, 7 covered through bounded on-demand tools, and 1 deliberately blocked gap — acquisition journey evidence, still dark until protected-customer-data approval is confirmed. The same work adds a Shopify intelligence tool layer for targeted order/product/customer context and analytical reads like sales mix, product performance, discounts, acquisition quality, returns, fulfilment, customer retention and action outcomes. Those tool results are transient reasoning evidence, not a Merchant Memory query log, and every packet carries `KNOWN`, `UNKNOWN`, `NOT_INGESTED`, `INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE` or `UNAVAILABLE` so Jefe cannot turn "we did not ingest this" into "this did not happen". `/health` now exposes the cheap catalog self-check. `app/lib/shopify/intelligence-coverage.server.js`, `app/lib/shopify/intelligence-tools.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-memory/commerce-analyst.server.js`, `app/routes/health.tsx`, `docs/ops/shopify-intelligence-coverage.md`, `tests/shopify-intelligence-coverage.test.mjs`, `tests/shopify-intelligence-tools.test.mjs`, `tests/commerce-analyst.test.mjs`.

- **Jefe can now tell where your orders actually come from — built, tested and deliberately still dark.** It could describe what you sold in enormous detail and never why anyone turned up, so "is the ad spend working" and "how much of this is just people who already know us" weren't questions it could engage with. Orders can now carry where the customer first arrived from — paid, search, social, email, referral or direct — with the campaign, referral code and a landing path, and a new belief reports the mix. Read from **first** touch on purpose: last-click flatters whatever sits nearest the checkout and would tell you your ads do nothing when the ads are what introduced the customer. A campaign that declares itself paid is always believed over a source that merely looks organic, because filing paid traffic as organic makes ad spend look free. **Nothing is requested until `ORDER_ATTRIBUTION_INGEST_ENABLED` is turned on** — the flag guards the query, not just the storage, because asking Shopify for a field the app isn't approved for fails the whole request and would take down order backfill for every store rather than just this. Two deliberate refusals: landing-page query strings are dropped at ingest, since they routinely carry an email or a token and none of it is needed; and a store whose orders predate the flag gets silence rather than a confident "100% direct", which is what an ungated read of empty journeys would produce for a perfectly healthy shop. Needs no new permission — `read_orders`, already granted — but does need Shopify's protected-customer-data approval before the flag is worth turning on. `app/lib/shopify/queries.server.js`, `app/lib/ingestion/shopify/canonical.server.js`, `app/lib/ingestion/shopify/backfill.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-memory/shopify-derivations.server.js`, `prisma/schema.prisma`, `tests/order-attribution.test.mjs`.

- **Jefe now knows whether your repeat business is a few devoted regulars or a lot of people who bought twice.** It could say 30% of your customers came back; it couldn't say whether that was twelve people buying every fortnight or three hundred buying twice — and those are different businesses that want opposite things done about them. Your customer base now splits into one-time, returning and loyal buyers, with what each group is actually worth, so "loyal customers are 12% of people and 40% of revenue" is a thing Jefe can see. It also works out who has gone quiet — **measured against your store's own rhythm, never a fixed number of days**. A furniture buyer silent for three months is behaving completely normally; a fortnightly coffee customer silent for three months has gone, and any fixed window is wrong about one of them. Where there aren't enough repeat customers to establish a rhythm, Jefe says the recency read is unavailable rather than reporting nobody has lapsed — "we can't tell yet" and "everyone's still here" are different, and only one of them means do nothing. Needs no new data and no new permissions: it reads the hashed customer records Jefe already builds from your orders, and stays aggregate — counts, shares and totals, never a name. `app/lib/merchant-memory/shopify-derivations.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-memory/deterministic-belief-registry.server.js`, `tests/customer-cohort-mix.test.mjs`.

- **Jefe now knows which offer discounted an order, not just that something did.** It could always say a store gives away 14% of its gross revenue in discounts — and never say what to do about it, because the number is the same whether customers are redeeming a summer code or everything has quietly been 10% off since March. Those are opposite facts: one is a campaign with a response rate, the other is margin leaving without anyone deciding it should. Orders now carry the codes customers typed and the automatic, manual and script discounts nobody typed, and a new belief reports which offers are doing the work and what share of discounted orders were a deliberate redemption. **Deliberately refuses to answer on old data**: orders ingested before today have no offer recorded, which at the database level is indistinguishable from "this order wasn't discounted" — so a store whose history predates the change gets silence rather than a confident and completely wrong "runs no promotions", until a re-backfill fills it in. Found by mapping the full Shopify Admin schema against what Jefe actually asks for: we use about 14 of 268 available queries, and 27 of the 127 fields on an order. `app/lib/shopify/queries.server.js`, `app/lib/ingestion/shopify/canonical.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-memory/shopify-derivations.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-memory/deterministic-belief-registry.server.js`, `prisma/schema.prisma`, `docs/ops/shopify-schema-coverage-2026-08-13.md`, `tests/discount-code-mix.test.mjs`.

- **Every wait is now timed from the moment the merchant acts — pressing enter, or saying yes.** Follow-up to the turn timing below, on the founder's call that the clock should start when the merchant does something, not at whichever internal boundary was convenient.
