Each now states execution is live and _inert only until a store has costed dead stock + a non-`recommend` dial_, not "dark." Historical CHANGELOG entries are left untouched (true as written). Docs-only; no behaviour change.

### Security

- **Event retention enabled (Level-2 pre-submit) + readiness synced.** `ENABLE_EVENT_RETENTION=true` set in production with a **180-day** window (`EVENT_RETENTION_ACTIVITY_DAYS` / `EVENT_RETENTION_USAGE_DAYS = 180`); `pruneOldEvents` (worker tick) now enforces retention on the `activity_events` + `llm_usage_event` telemetry logs only (never Merchant Memory beliefs). Closes the Level-2 "Retention + deletion" commitment. Also synced `docs/ops/level-2-readiness.md` (encryption-in-transit ✅ `sslmode=require` verified in prod; retention ✅; IR ✅; compliance webhooks ✅) and filled `docs/ops/incident-response.md` `[CONFIRM]` items (named roles, notification timelines, postmortem + annual-review cadence) at the founder's delegation. Remaining Level-2 item before submit: #10 privacy-copy accuracy (chat 6). (#5 ops-panel access-logging + `OPS_PUBLIC` removal landed in parallel — chat 8, `0bf4f04`.)

## 2026-07-30

### Performance

- **`app._index` decomposition — move 1: `DailyHome` is now code-split.** The returning-user home (`DailyHome`, ~35kB client / 50kB server, hand-rolled, zero Polaris) renders **only** for `appMode === "daily"`, yet it was statically imported into the 4.6k-line `app._index` route module — so **every first-run onboarding and memory-view user downloaded it for nothing**. It's now `React.lazy` + `Suspense`; because the app streams SSR, the server still resolves and streams the real `DailyHome` HTML for daily users (React selective hydration keeps that server markup in place — no fallback flash, no CLS, LCP preserved). Net: the `app._index` route chunk drops **85.8 → 51.5 kB raw (21.8 → 13.9 kB gzip)** and `DailyHome` splits into its own **35.4 kB (8.83 kB gzip)** chunk fetched only in daily mode. Behaviour-preserving, no schema change; full gate green (0 failures) and the build confirms the split. First move of the perf-first `app._index` decomposition (addresses the returning-user LCP/interaction pain). `app/routes/app._index.tsx`. Next moves: code-split the onboarding step components and lazy-load `MerchantMemoryView`, each coordinated with the session that owns that region.

### Removed

- **Removed the bespoke cost-sheet parser** (`app/lib/ingestion/merchant-cost-sheet.server.js` + `tests/merchant-cost-sheet.test.mjs`), per founder direction: merchant data — including costs — ingests via **one general upload → LLM-extract pipeline**, not per-format parsers. The parser was **unwired** (no callers across `apps/shopify/app`), so this is a clean removal. The **Shopify-native cost path** (`cost_per_item` read from Shopify's own field) is untouched. This also drops the earlier same-day European-number-format enhancement to that parser — no loss, it was against the direction. Stale "cost-sheet modality" references in `docs/merchant-memory-state.md` corrected to point at the general pipeline.

### Added

- **Install/uninstall Slack pings link the Jefe ops merchant view.** The shop domain in each #jefe-slack lifecycle line now links to `admin.mynamejefe.com/merchant?shop=<domain>` (the ops drill-down: net revenue, margin, COGS coverage, churn reason, activity) — the high-value “who just (un)installed?” click — with a secondary `store ↗` link to the actual storefront. `OPS_APP_URL`-overridable, prod default baked in. `app/lib/observability/lifecycle-slack.server.js`.

- **Install/uninstall Slack pings now link the storefront.** The shop domain in each #jefe-slack lifecycle line is a clickable Slack link (`<https://{shop}|{shop}>`) to the store, so a ping is one click from seeing who it is (was plain bold). `app/lib/observability/lifecycle-slack.server.js`.

- **Install / uninstall Slack pings to #jefe-slack.** We now get a lifecycle line in the ops Slack the moment a shop installs (🎉 / 🔄 reinstall) or uninstalls (👋, with tenure). Reuses the existing `ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL` webhook (same channel as the changelog watcher + error alerts) — no new config, no-op if unset. **Install is gated on the genuine (re)install transition:** `afterAuth` reads the shop's status _before_ the backfill queue reactivates it, so a routine re-auth / token refresh / scope change doesn't ping (only a new shop or a previously inactive/uninstalled one does). Uninstall fires from the `app/uninstalled` webhook on the genuine first delivery, never a redelivery. Both are fire-and-forget + self-catching, so a Slack hiccup can never delay or break install/auth/uninstall. Goes to our own internal channel (shop domain only, no customer PII). New `app/lib/observability/lifecycle-slack.server.js` (`notifyShopLifecycleToSlack` + pure `formatLifecycleText`, 8 tests); wired in `app/shopify.server.ts` (afterAuth) + `app/lib/ingestion/shopify/webhooks.server.js` (uninstall).

- **Scope-gated-opportunity signal — the value-first re-consent nudge.** Adds `requiredScopes` to the Action Capability Registry (clearance → `write_products`) + `getRequiredScopes(actionType)`, and a new read `getScopeGatedOpportunity(prisma, { merchantId, shopId, currency })` that fires **only when Jefe has a valuable action ready but the merchant hasn't granted the Shopify scope it needs to execute** — returning a value-led headline (_"Jefe found £9,402 tied up in 4 products it can clear for you — grant 'Edit products' access to let it act"_) plus the missing scopes + money. It's null when there's no opportunity, the scope is already granted, or the action needs no write scope. This is the trigger both the **in-app re-consent prompt** and the **activation email** fire on — value-driven, not a bare "grant more access" ask — and the pattern every future write-action reuses. My lane (registry + read); Chat 2 owns the email/prompt UI, chat 10 the grant redirect + the execution-gate precheck (`getRequiredScopes` is there for it). `app/lib/actions/{action-intent,action-resolution}.server.js`; unit-tested (gated → signal, granted → null, no-opportunity → null). Dormant-safe: reads only.

- **Close the learn loop — clearance markdown adapts from decline memory.** The `price_markdown` resolver now reads the merchant's `business.action_decline_signal.all_time` belief: when past clearances were repeatedly declined as _too aggressive_ (≥2, top reason), Jefe eases the **default** markdown (30% → 20%, floored at 15%) so the next proposal is gentler — the Observe→Learn "learn" step, closing the loop deterministically at the proposal params (not a plan-quality-sensitive prompt change). Only ever _eases_ (the safe direction); the primitive still floors every price at unit cost; an explicit markdown (LLM or merchant edit) is always respected — memory adapts **only** the default. Best-effort read (never blocks a proposal); a no-op until real declines exist. `app/lib/actions/action-resolution.server.js`; 3 new tests. (The _plan-rec-prompt_-level loop-closing — the LLM weighing effectiveness/decline beliefs when deciding whether to propose at all — is deliberately deferred for founder review + real post-live data.)
- **Autonomy-as-policy — per-action-type caps that govern the "autonomous" mode.** New `policy` JSONB column on `action_autonomy_policies` (migration `20260731020016`) + `getActionPolicy` / `setActionPolicy` + a pure `applyAutonomyPolicy(resolved, policy, context)`. On top of the 3-mode dial, a merchant can cap what Jefe auto-executes per action type — `autoMaxTrappedCapital` (£ tied up), `autoMaxVariants`, `autoMaxDiscountPercent` — i.e. _"clear dead stock autonomously up to £X"_ (Matt's governance model: broad policies, not a settings page of minute caps). It only ever **constrains**: an `auto` run over a cap degrades to `approve`-first (asks the merchant), naming the violated cap; `recommend` / `approve` pass through untouched; an empty policy is a no-op; and the structural safety caps (blast-radius, floor-at-cost) still apply underneath, always. Wired into `proposeActionFromIntent` so the persisted `resolvedMode` reflects the policy — chat 10's execution gate reads that mode, so no execution-side change. Additive/reversible, reads only. `app/lib/actions/action-autonomy-policy.server.js` + resolution wiring; 4 new tests. The merchant-facing settings UI (which caps to expose + defaults) is Chat 2's lane + a founder call.

- **Benchmark-priors provenance class — cold-start / comparison scaffolding (no data yet).** New `benchmark-priors.server.js` making benchmark priors a **distinct provenance class** from Merchant Memory: `asBenchmarkPrior` stamps every prior with `provenance:"benchmark_prior"` + a hard `isMerchantFact:false`, so no surface or LLM can mistake a peer benchmark for the merchant's own fact (and a merchant correction can never "correct" a prior — it isn't theirs). `compareToBenchmark(merchantValue, prior, { higherIsBetter })` is the comparison primitive (_"your dead-stock share is 45% vs a 30% benchmark"_ → above/below + better/worse), and `isSurfaceableBenchmark` gates on a ≥20-store cohort so a thin benchmark never misleads. Aggregate + PII-safe by construction (a prior describes a **segment**, never a store or a customer). Ships with **no data and no consumer wired** — the actual prior values are gated on the founder's read of the Quiver benchmark DB (contents + confirming it's aggregate-safe); this is the typed rules the data plugs into. `app/lib/merchant-memory/benchmark-priors.server.js`; 3 tests.

- **Memory surface gets per-belief `authorship` + `confirmState` + `sourceLine`.** `getMerchantMemoryView` beliefs now carry `authorship` (`"merchant"` = confirmed/corrected vs `"jefe"` = system-inferred — drives the surface's grouping + left-rule), `confirmState` (`"settled"` = merchant-owned or a confident inference vs `"unsure"` = a low-confidence one — drives Edit/Forget vs "That's right / Not quite"), and a short `sourceLine` provenance (_"you told Jefe · 29 Jul"_ / _"from your store data · rechecked 31 Jul"_). All deterministic from `status` / `confidence` / dates. These are exactly the optional fields chat 11's 13a app-home Memory redesign reads (it falls back honestly when absent), so this lights them up. The plain-English `statement` rendering — the handoff's "Memory must _say_ things, not name fields" gap — is a per-belief-key pass and is deliberately **not** in this change. `app/lib/merchant-memory/service.server.js`; 2 tests. For chat 11 (surface consumer).

- **Daily Home "What Jefe did" feed — the surface for the Observe end of the loop.** Renders chat 9's `getExecutedActionFeed` in the Brief (below the suggested action): each executed clearance with its measured outcome ("9 of 12 cleared products sold · 20 units moved · £810 recovered"), or "Jefe's tracking the results" while a run is still being scored. **Honest by construction (chat 7's bar):** only `applied` / `partially_applied` / `reverted` rows exist server-side (failed omitted); the headline reflects what ACTUALLY landed (not the proposed count); a `reverted` run renders muted as "Attempted, then rolled back automatically — nothing was left changed", never a success. All strings are server-formatted in the shop currency and rendered as-is. The whole section **self-hides when the feed is empty**, so it's invisible until `CLEARANCE_EXECUTE_ENABLED` is on and Jefe has actually done something — zero visible change on deploy. Completes the merchant-facing clearance loop: suggest → approve/decline/edit → **see what Jefe did**. `app/components/daily-home.tsx` (`ExecutedActionFeed` + type), `app/routes/app._index.tsx` (daily loader wiring + prop). Coordinated with chat 9 (the read) + chat 7 (the honesty rules). Also updates the design backlog premise (P1 #4 was "no action log" — now built).

- **`getExecutedActionFeed` — the read behind the Daily Home "what Jefe did for you" feed.** A new read off the `action_executions` ledger (same server-formatted contract as `getActiveSuggestedAction`) returning the merchant's executed-action history — applied / partially-applied / reverted rows, most recent first — each with the action type, a short applied-change headline, a timestamp, and the **measured outcome** (units moved / cash recovered / effectiveness rate) once the Observe step has scored it (`outcome.measured` is `false` until then). Money is formatted server-side in the shop currency; the surface renders the strings as-is. Empty until execution is live (nothing is applied while the write flag is off). `app/lib/actions/action-resolution.server.js`; unit-tested. Coordinated: chat 2 (builds the "while you slept" surface to this shape).

- **Public "What's new" page — built, shipped DARK behind `ENABLE_PUBLIC_CHANGELOG`.** A merchant-facing product-update page at `/whats-new`, written in Jefe's voice — NOT the engineer-facing CHANGELOG. First-pass copy is deliberately conservative + honest: it describes only what a merchant can see today and frames the action layer as advisory ("Jefe suggests… you approve, adjust, or decline") — never claims Jefe acts on its own while execution is gated. Public, unauthenticated, `noindex`; **404s until a human flips the flag**, so it deploys without being exposed. `app/routes/whats-new.tsx`. **HELD for founder (1-way):** reviewing/editing the copy (it's your brand voice), then flipping the flag + linking it — publishing a public page is your call. Resolves the "public-ready changelog page" follow-up.

- **Registered the mandatory GDPR/privacy compliance webhooks** (App Store submission requirement). The handlers for `customers/redact`, `customers/data_request`, and `shop/redact` already existed and verify HMAC (via `handleShopifyWebhookRequest`, covered by the compliance tests), but they were **not declared** in `shopify.app.toml` — so Shopify wouldn't subscribe them, which fails App Store review (and the automated common-error check). Added the `[webhooks.privacy_compliance]` block mapping the three mandatory topics to the existing routes; takes effect on the next `shopify app deploy`. No handler change — this is registration only. `shopify.app.toml`.

- **Decline-signal belief — memory learns _what/why_ merchants reject.** New deterministic belief `business.action_decline_signal.all_time` (registry 131→132) that aggregates the PII-safe `merchant_action_declined` events into decline counts by **reason category** and action type, with the most common reason and its share. This lets the plan-rec adapt — a merchant who keeps declining clearances as "too aggressive" wants gentler markdowns next time. `direct_observation_v1` confidence, gated on **≥3 declines**; **reason categories only** (the free-text note is already redacted at write, and the derivation never reads it). Dormant until actions are executable (declines only flow once the write flag is on). Derivation + guarded context loader (reads `activity_events`), registry def (new "Action feedback v1" tranche), new fixture test; full gate green. Completes the "a decline always carries a reason, and that reason is how Jefe learns" loop the surface promised.

- **Server error-report policy extracted + unit-tested (`handleError` → Sentry).** Which server errors reach Sentry/alerts vs. are skipped as expected non-faults (aborted requests, 4xx route responses) was decided inline and untested in `entry.server.tsx`. Pulled it into a pure `shouldReportServerError(error, { aborted })` (`error-policy.server.js`) and locked it with `tests/observability-error-policy.test.mjs`: aborted → skip (even a 5xx), 4xx route responses → skip, **5xx + genuine exceptions → report, and anything unclassified reports (fail open — better a little noise than a dropped fault)**. Behaviour-identical refactor of the server entry; complements the SDK-level `isBenignForSentry` backstop — the two are layered (`handleError` decides whether to capture, `beforeSend` filters what's captured, incl. Sentry auto-instrumentation). `app/entry.server.tsx`, `app/lib/observability/error-policy.server.js`, `tests/observability-error-policy.test.mjs`.

- **Clearance action handlers are now observable.** The four merchant action intents (`action.approve` / `action.reject` / `action.edit` / `action.set_mode`) previously fired their service call and redirected, silently discarding the result — so a decline that hit an already-acted row, or a set-mode rejected as invalid, left no trace. Each now captures the outcome and logs it through the structured logger (`component: "action"`): **info** on success, **warn** on a non-applied result (`not_proposable`, `not_revisable`, `invalid_mode`, …), with merchant + run identifiers, the autonomy mode, and — for approve — whether a real store write actually `executed` (flag on) vs. was recorded dark (flag off). **PII-safe by construction:** the decline's `reasonCategory` (a fixed enum) is logged but the merchant's free-text note is never logged. Closes the observability definition-of-done for Jefe's first real action. `app/routes/app._index.tsx`.

- **Scheduled clearance-outcome measurement + belief refresh — the loop now _runs_.** The backfill worker tick now calls `measureAndRecordClearanceOutcomes` once per UTC day (self-catching, in-memory day-guard at hour 6; on by default, opt out with `ENABLE_CLEARANCE_OUTCOME_JOB=false`), then enqueues a Merchant Memory refresh (`business` category) for each merchant with newly-measured runs so the `clearance_effectiveness` belief updates automatically. This turns the Observe→Learn cycle from inert (the loader existed but nothing called it) into a running loop. Still **dormant in prod** — no applied runs while `CLEARANCE_EXECUTE_ENABLED` is off, so it measures nothing and enqueues nothing — but the moment the flag flips, the first real clearance is scored within a day and memory learns from it with no further wiring. Structured logging on the worker (`backfill-worker` component); idempotent (only `pending` rows scored, so a worker restart can't double-count). `app/services/shopify-backfill-worker.server.js`; also tightened the loader's types now that it's imported into the app graph (`app/lib/actions/clearance-outcome.server.js`). Full gate green.

- **Clearance-effectiveness belief — the Observe→Learn "Learn" step.** New deterministic Merchant Memory belief `business.clearance_effectiveness.all_time` (registry 130→131) that aggregates the measured outcomes on the `action_executions` ledger: variants cleared/sold, units moved, revenue recovered, plus variant sell-through and per-run effectiveness rates. Mirrors `recommendation_engagement` exactly — a guarded context loader (reads `action_executions` where `outcomeStatus="measured"`), `direct_observation_v1` confidence, gated on **≥3 measured runs**. This is the "did the action actually work" signal on the earned-autonomy ramp: a merchant whose clearances consistently move stock is one Jefe can act for with less friction. **Dormant until execution is live** (there are no measured runs while `CLEARANCE_EXECUTE_ENABLED` is off); it lights up the moment the first real clearance is scored by `measureAndRecordClearanceOutcomes`. Derivation + context field in `app/lib/merchant-memory/shopify-derivations.server.js`, registry def, new fixture test; full gate green (436/436). Together with the outcome-measurement loader this closes the belief → decision → action → **outcome → belief** loop in code, dark behind the flag.

- **Win-back CAMPAIGN (emails 2 & 3) — built, shipped DARK behind `ENABLE_WINBACK_CAMPAIGN`.** Extends the single Day-0 farewell into a short, self-terminating sequence that keeps reaching out to churned shops **until we get a "why" (feedback) or they reinstall**, then stops — the founder-requested win-back campaign. Safety-first by construction: **hard cap of 3 emails ever**, four exit conditions re-checked before every send (`campaignStopReason`: reinstall / feedback / unsubscribe), one-click RFC-8058 unsubscribe on each, per-step atomic claims (`updateMany WHERE …_sent_at IS NULL`) so a step can't double-send, and a per-tick batch cap. Runs on the existing worker tick (`maybeSendWinBackCampaign`, alongside the changelog watcher — Railway infra, no new dependency). Cadence day 0 / 4 / 10 as tunable constants; emails 2 (light curiosity nudge) & 3 (last note + soft reconnect) reuse the Day-0 design system + the same signed one-tap feedback/unsubscribe links. New: `app/lib/email/winback-campaign.server.js`, templates `jefe-winback-2.html` / `jefe-winback-3.html`, migration `20260730170000_winback_campaign_steps` (additive: `winback_email2_sent_at`, `winback_email3_sent_at`, `winback_recipient_email`), 14 tests. **Recipient persistence:** the uninstall webhook deletes the shop's Session rows, so the follow-up recipient is persisted at Day-0 send time — but ONLY when the campaign flag is on, so a **dark deploy stores nothing new and sends nothing**. **HELD for founder (1-way doors, NOT done here):** (1) softening Day-0's live close ("No emails after this one…"), which the follow-ups would otherwise contradict; (2) confirming cadence/copy; (3) flipping `ENABLE_WINBACK_CAMPAIGN`. `app/lib/email/winback.server.js` (persist recipient + clear the full sequence on reinstall), `app/services/shopify-backfill-worker.server.js` (tick wiring). Founder note: persisting the churned merchant's own email at rest is new — reversible (drop the column), gated, opt-out-honoured; flag if you'd rather not.

- **Edit a suggestion — adjust the markdown %, Jefe re-proposes.** The "Jefe suggests" card now carries an **Adjust markdown · N%** control alongside Approve/Decline (shown when the action is executable). It reveals a small inline input defaulted to Jefe's proposed % (`SuggestedAction.markdownPercent`, surfaced by chat 9's `getActiveSuggestedAction`); the merchant sets a new number and **Re-propose** POSTs `action.edit {actionRunId, markdownPercent}` → `reviseAction`, which re-runs the proposal at that %, **still floored at unit cost by the primitive** (the merchant suggests; the safety math is never overridden), supersedes the old proposed row, and the loader re-reads the fresh proposal so the card updates with the new numbers + runId. No external write — this is the propose half. Completes the _edit_ side of the founder-requested "let merchants edit or decline our suggestions, with a reason, so we learn" (decline-with-reason shipped earlier). Inert until a proposed row exists + executable (flag on). `app/components/daily-home.tsx` (the `EditMarkdownForm` + the Adjust affordance), `app/routes/app._index.tsx` (the `action.edit` handler). Coordinated with chat 9 (owns `reviseAction` + the `markdownPercent` field; I own the control + handler).

- **Clearance Observe step — the outcome-measurement loader.** `measureAndRecordClearanceOutcomes` (`app/lib/actions/clearance-outcome.server.js`) scores applied clearance runs whose measurement window has elapsed: it gathers each cleared variant's post-clearance sales, runs the pure `measureClearanceOutcome`, and persists the result on the `action_executions` row (`outcome` + `outcomeStatus="measured"` + `outcomeMeasuredAt`). Idempotent — it only picks up `pending` rows and flips them to `measured`, so a re-run never double-scores; a run with no cleared variants is still marked measured (zeros) so it can't re-queue forever. No external write, only the ledger row is updated. **Dormant until execution is live** (there are no applied runs while `CLEARANCE_EXECUTE_ENABLED` is off) — the ready-for-flip "Observe" half of the belief → decision → action → **outcome** loop, so the _first_ real clearance is measured the moment the flag flips. Covered by mock-prisma tests (query targets only unscored/applied/past-window runs; outcome persisted onto the row). Full gate green (435/435). Next in the lane: the effectiveness belief that aggregates the measured rows into Merchant Memory (the "Learn" step) + the scheduled job that runs this.

- **Decline reason now captured as split category + note (Observe→Learn signal sharpened).** The suggested-action **Decline** flow already collected a category chip + an optional note; the `action.reject` handler was joining them into one `reason` string. It now passes them as **separate** `reasonCategory` + `reasonText` fields to `rejectAction`, which records them un-joined on the PII-safe `merchant_action_declined` event — so the action-feedback corpus can group declines by category without parsing. Behaviour-identical for the merchant (same picker); the learning signal is just cleaner. Completes the reason-split half of the decline/edit contract with the action-engine lane (chat 9). Categories / merchant-typed notes only, never customer data. `app/routes/app._index.tsx`.

- **Hardened the Sentry drop-filter into a tested contract + covered alerting's storm-recovery.** The `beforeSend` drop-decision shipped earlier today (drop benign SSR aborts + 4xx, keep real faults) lived inline in the SDK-init closure and was untested at the decision level — and an over-broad filter's failure mode is _silently dropping real errors_, exactly what Sentry exists to catch. Extracted it to a pure, exported `isBenignForSentry(originalException)` and locked the contract with `tests/sentry-filter.test.mjs`: benign stream aborts + 4xx (incl. the 400/499 boundaries) drop; **real 5xx, status-less errors, and unknown/empty exceptions are kept**. Also covered the one alerting path that wasn't — the per-minute cap **resets** after the window elapses, so alerting recovers after a storm instead of staying silenced (`observability-alerting.test.mjs`). Behaviour-preserving refactor; full gate green. `app/lib/observability/sentry.server.js`, `tests/sentry-filter.test.mjs`, `tests/observability-alerting.test.mjs`.

- **Sentry noise filter — benign SSR aborts + bot-POST 4xx no longer clutter Sentry.** `sentry.server.js`'s `beforeSend` now drops the errors that are already logged at WARN and aren't faults: a client disconnecting mid-SSR-stream (`isBenignStreamError` — "destination stream errored", `ERR_STREAM_PREMATURE_CLOSE`, `EPIPE`, `ECONNRESET`) and 4xx route responses (bot POSTs to actionless routes, 404s/405s). Filtering at the SDK level (not only in `handleError`) also catches Sentry's own auto-instrumentation, so a _real_ error stands out instead of drowning in disconnect noise. Cleaned up the 2 stale benign issues sitting in Sentry (both were "last seen 1d ago", now resolved). `app/lib/observability/sentry.server.js`; `isBenignStreamError` now covered by `tests/stream-errors.test.mjs`.

- **Jefe's first action goes end-to-end: the plan-rec now _emits_ a clearance proposal, and the Daily Home card renders it.** Closes the last gap before Jefe's first live action — execution stays dark behind `CLEARANCE_EXECUTE_ENABLED` (Matt's go-live flip). Four pieces:
  - **The emit (Jefe decides _from memory_).** The Merchant Plan LLM may now attach an optional, registry-validated `actionIntent` to its recommendation (`app/lib/merchant-plan/{schema,prompt}.server.js`): it picks the _verb_ from the Action Capability Registry, and only when supplied memory supports acting now — the magnitude (e.g. `markdownPercent`) is advisory. After the plan commits, `service.server.js` hands any intent to `proposeActionFromIntent`, which **re-resolves it against live memory and computes the floored/capped params itself**, creating a `proposed` `ActionExecution` row _only_ when there's a real, safe opportunity — writing **nothing** external. Best-effort + **outside** the plan transaction: an emit failure never fails plan generation; an unknown/malformed intent is dropped (the plan still stands); a spurious emit with no dead stock is a no-op. This is Matt's "LLM picks the verb, the primitive computes safe params" — no per-action shaper.
  - **Money on the row (render at read time, no re-query).** `proposeActionFromIntent` now persists a `proposalSummary` (trapped capital, projected recovery, top items with units — summed over the _surviving_ preview set so money agrees with the count) on the row: new **nullable, additive** `proposal_summary` JSONB column (migration `20260730155040_action_execution_proposal_summary`).
  - **The card binding.** New `getActiveSuggestedAction(prisma, { merchantId, shopId, currency })` reads the latest `proposed` row into the render-ready `SuggestedAction`, formatting money **server-side in the shop currency** (the card renders the strings as-is). Wired into the `app._index` daily loader (`suggestedAction: null` → the real object). `executable` is true only when a real proposed row exists **and** `CLEARANCE_EXECUTE_ENABLED` is on **and** the mode isn't recommend-only — so the card stays honestly advisory until the flip.
  - **Edit + reason split.** New `reviseAction` (re-propose at a merchant-suggested markdown — still floored/capped by the primitive — and supersede the original: the _edit a suggestion_ half), and `rejectAction`'s reason is now split into `reasonCategory` + `reasonText` on the `merchant_action_declined` signal (a legacy joined `reason` is still accepted during the surface migration).
    All deterministic math stays in code; the LLM only picks the verb. New unit tests (`formatMoney`, `buildProposalSummary`, `getActiveSuggestedAction`, `reviseAction`, the split decline event, `actionIntent` normalization) + a **DB-gated end-to-end test** proving _plan → actionIntent → proposed clearance row_ (`tests/{action-resolution,merchant-plan}.test.mjs`). Full gate green (**424/424 with DB**). Wired via a worktree off origin/main. Coordinated: **chat 2** (drop the reason-join → pass `reasonCategory` + `reasonText`; wire `action.edit` → `reviseAction`; the loader `suggestedAction` binding is now live), **chat 7** (a new `superseded` `ActionExecution` status for revisions; the flag-OFF dry-run before the go-live flip).

- **Decline now asks _why_ — the reason picker that feeds Observe→Learn.** The "Jefe suggests" card's **Decline** no longer rejects silently: it opens a small in-card prompt — a category picker (**Too aggressive · Wrong products · Bad timing · Already handled it · Other**) plus an optional one-line note — and posts the chosen reason with `action.reject`. The `app._index` handler joins category + note into one string and hands it to chat 4's `rejectAction(prisma, { merchantId, actionRunId, reason })`, which emits the PII-safe `merchant_action_declined` learning signal. **Categories / a merchant-typed note only — never customer data**: the picker exposes no PII field and the note is capped at 140 chars as a backstop. Completes the _decline_ half of the founder-requested "let merchants edit or decline our suggestions, with a reason, so we learn" (the _edit_ half — revise the markdown % → re-propose — is next, with chat 4). Still inert until the `SuggestedAction` is bound (the card renders only when `executable && actionRunId`), so zero live change on deploy. `app/components/daily-home.tsx` (new `DeclineReasonForm`), `app/routes/app._index.tsx` (the `action.reject` handler now passes `reason`). Full gate green. Coordinated: chat 4 (owns `rejectAction` + the reason contract — category / short text, never PII).

- **Clearance action surface wired — Approve→execute, Decline, and the 3-mode autonomy dial.** The merchant-facing half of Jefe's first real action. The generic "suggested action" card now carries the execution controls: **Approve →** posts `action.approve` → chat 7's `wireClearanceExecution(prisma, session, { merchantId, actionRunId, mode: "approve" })` (records the approval, then — ONLY if `CLEARANCE_EXECUTE_ENABLED` — loads the offline token, builds the live Shopify client, and runs the reversible `applyClearance`); **Decline** posts `action.reject` → chat 4's `rejectAction`; and a **3-mode picker** (Recommend / I approve each / Autonomous) posts `action.set_mode` → chat 4's `setActionMode` for `price_markdown` — the per-merchant×action-type autonomy dial `resolveAutonomyMode` reads at propose-time. Honest by construction: Approve/Decline render only when `executable && actionRunId`, and with the write-flag OFF the whole path is a **safe no-op** (records the decision, no store write) — so it ships **dark** behind `CLEARANCE_EXECUTE_ENABLED` (Matt's go-live flip, after a first-live test round-trip). Still inert until chat 4 binds the `SuggestedAction` (loader `suggestedAction: null` → the real object with `actionRunId`/`mode`), so zero live change on deploy. `app/components/daily-home.tsx` (card + picker + `SuggestedAction` fields), `app/routes/app._index.tsx` (action handlers). My files typecheck + lint + build clean; wired via a worktree off origin/main (the shared main checkout was stuck on an unpushed schema commit). Coordinated: chat 7 (`wireClearanceExecution` + write client), chat 4 (`rejectAction` + `setActionMode` + the `SuggestedAction` binding).

- **Trimmed OAuth scopes to the 7 actually used** (`shopify.app.toml`) — dropped `write_orders`, `write_customers`, `write_inventory`, `write_locations` (verified unused: the only write mutation in the app is `productVariantsBulkUpdate` for clearance, which needs `write_products`; everything else is read-path). New set: `read_products, write_products, read_orders, read_all_orders, read_customers, read_inventory, read_locations`. **Request only what you use** — unused write scopes are an App-Store-review flag; scopes grow per-action as the Action Capability Registry ships new write primitives (each adds its scope, re-consented at that point). **Takes effect on the next `shopify app deploy`** (folded into the pre-submit deploy; reducing scopes needs no merchant re-consent). Coordinated with chat 6 (syncs the listing install-requirements + `legal-triggers.json` to this set).
- Added the **clearance approve→execute orchestrator** (`wireClearanceExecution`) + its **live Shopify write client** — the last code-piece before clearance go-live. `wireClearanceExecution(prisma, session, { merchantId, actionRunId, mode })` is the **single entry point** the surface calls for both the merchant-approve (`mode:"approve"`) and autonomous (`mode:"auto"`) paths: it records the approval (proposed→approved) **inline** — folded into this one fn per the 3-mode model, since there's no approve-without-execute — then the flag-gated `applyClearance` does the store write. Safety gates run **before** any write: the row's `resolvedMode` is authoritative (a `recommend` row never executes; a `mode:"auto"` call is refused unless the merchant's setting resolved to `auto` — the caller can't widen what the merchant authorized), empty previews are refused, and re-fires are idempotent (`already_applied`). The **live write client** (`createClearanceShopifyClient`) is the read+write pair the adapter injects — `getVariantPrice` (the compare-and-set source) + `updateVariantPrice` (`productVariantsBulkUpdate`), wrapping `ShopifyAdminGraphqlClient` with the shop's offline token. **Write-path observability (I2):** run-level info (start / complete), WARN on price-drift skips, ERROR on failure → auto-forwarded to the ops alerter (Slack). **Shipped UNWIRED + flag-gated:** nothing calls `wireClearanceExecution` yet, and the store write stays behind `CLEARANCE_EXECUTE_ENABLED` — **going live is a single deliberate step** (the surface wires the Approve button + the 3-mode picker to it, the merchant picks a non-`recommend` mode, and the flag is flipped). 16 tests (client 6 + orchestrator 10 — mock-prisma full stack incl. flag off/on, drift-skip, idempotency, auto-authorization); full gate green (345 pass). `app/lib/actions/wire-clearance-execution.server.js`, `app/lib/actions/clearance-shopify-client.server.js`. Coordinated: chat 2 (surface wiring + picker), chat 4 (proposal + `approveAction`). Go-live honesty gate: execution must be **live at App-Store-submit** for the "Jefe acts" claim to be true (chat 6).

- **Schema↔migration drift reconciled; CI drift guard is now a hard gate.** A real `prisma migrate diff` showed ~15 tables had drifted between the migration files and `schema.prisma` (redundant `updated_at` DB-defaults vs `@updatedAt`, Prisma index-name changes, 2 `SET NOT NULL`, a few nullability tweaks) — accumulated from schema edits made without matching migrations. Reconciled in one migration `20260730140000_reconcile_schema_drift` (38 statements) so a fresh `migrate deploy` now matches `schema.prisma` exactly. Verified end-to-end (fresh `postgres:16`, all migrations applied in order → `migrate diff --exit-code` = 0) and prod-safe (the 2 `SET NOT NULL` columns — `inventory_levels.inventory_item_external_id`, `order_line_items.external_id` — have **0 nulls** in prod, tables are ≤54 rows, and prod's live schema matched the migration files so it applies identically). With drift now 0, the `ci.yml` drift check is flipped back to a **hard gate** (blocking), so any future drift is caught as a real regression. `apps/shopify/prisma/migrations/20260730140000_reconcile_schema_drift/`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.
- Added the generic **"suggested action" advisory card** to Daily Home — the render building block for Jefe's first _visible_ action (and every action after), deliberately **NOT** bound to a per-action shaper. This matches the autonomy-from-install direction (Matt, 2026-07-30): one action ontology — the LLM proposes actions from memory, typed primitives execute — not a bespoke clearance-card/reorder-card/… per action. It renders a generic `{ headline, keyNumbers, topItems, executable }` shape as a "Jefe suggests" card in the Brief's "Your call", below the Plan recommendation, only when an action is present. **`executable: false`** (execution still dark) → advisory only, **no live Approve button** (honest — the #17 principle: never render a control that can't work); **`executable: true`** → the real **Approve → execute** trigger appears (the route `action.approve` handler + typed reversible adapter is the execution lane, chat 4). Shipped **inert** via a `suggestedAction: null` loader seam — the card stays hidden until chat 4 binds the data source (LLM plan-rec + typed primitive), so this deploys with zero live change. `app/components/daily-home.tsx` (component + exported `SuggestedAction` type) + the `app._index` daily loader/render seam. Full gate green (369/369). Coordinated: chat 4 (owns the binding + execution), chat 7 (cleared the daily-branch edit — separate region from their MemoryView/step extractions).
- Added **decline-with-reason capture** to the action layer — when a merchant declines a proposed action, `rejectAction` now takes an optional `reason` and emits a PII-safe `merchant_action_declined` activity event (`buildActionDeclinedEvent`: actionType + reason + runId). A declined suggestion _and why_ is an Observe→Learn signal as valuable as an outcome — it sharpens which suggestions to make and feeds earned autonomy. Reason is a category / short text, never customer data; same activity-stream pattern as intent-capture, no schema change. 8 tests. `app/lib/actions/action-resolution.server.js` (surface: the Decline prompt passes the reason). Part of the founder-requested "let merchants edit or decline our suggestions, with a reason, so we learn" — the _edit_ half (revise the proposal, e.g. markdown %) is the resolution re-run + a surface control, coordinating with chat 2.
- Added the **autonomy-dial policy store** — the merchant's mode per action-type (`recommend` / `approve_execute` / `autonomous`) that drives whether Jefe auto-runs, proposes, or stays advisory. `setActionMode` / `getActionMode` (`action-autonomy-policy.server.js`; defaults `approve_execute` = propose-first, **never auto by default**; refuses unknown modes), and the resolution layer now reads the merchant's per-action-type dial instead of a hardcoded default (proven by a test: `autonomous` + eligible → resolvedMode `auto`). This is the storage the surface's 3-mode picker sets/reads via `setActionMode`/`getActionMode`. Also **resolves a pre-existing schema drift**: `action_autonomy_policies` was created by an earlier migration (`reconcile_schema_drift`) but had no Prisma model — the model is now added, matching the table exactly, so **no new migration** (a duplicate CREATE would break the deploy; c5_gate caught it). 6 policy tests + resolution test, typecheck green. `app/lib/actions/action-autonomy-policy.server.js`, `prisma/schema.prisma`.
- Added **`rejectAction`** to the action-resolution layer — the merchant's decline on a proposed action (proposed→rejected; a memory/engagement signal, no store write). **Approval is not a standalone step:** in the 3-mode autonomy model there's no approve-without-execute (recommend has no approve; approve_execute + autonomous both execute), so the approve→execute transition is owned by the execution-contract side (`wireClearanceExecution`) as one fn — the surface calls that, not a separate approve. Guarded (only a `proposed` row, only its own merchant). `app/lib/actions/action-resolution.server.js`.
- Added the **action-resolution layer** (`action-resolution.server.js`) — the middle of the kinetic path. `proposeActionFromIntent` turns a validated LLM action-intent into a deterministic, previewed, ledger-recorded **proposal** + the `SuggestedAction` the surface renders: validate → dispatch to the primitive's resolver (`price_markdown` → dead-stock proposal, markdown floored at unit cost) → `buildClearancePreview` → structural eligibility → `resolveAutonomyMode` (the merchant's dial × the gate; v1 defaults to `approve_execute` = propose-first) → creates the **`proposed`** `ActionExecution` row (so the card gets a `runId`). **No external write** — this is the propose half; the merchant's approve + the gated adapter do the rest. Explicit `price_markdown` dispatch until a 2nd primitive. 4 tests (mock-prisma full flow, validation guard, no-opportunity), typecheck green; **unwired** — the plan-recommendation emit wires it next. `app/lib/actions/action-resolution.server.js`.
- Added the **action-intent contract + Action Capability Registry** (`action-intent.server.js`) — the typed seam between LLM advice and typed execution primitives, and the keystone of the generic action path. The LLM (the plan-recommendation, reading memory) emits an `{actionType, targetKind, params}` intent from a **fixed registered vocabulary**; a typed primitive then resolves the concrete target + computes the deterministic, safety-floored params **itself (never the LLM)**. First registered action: `price_markdown` (targets `dead_stock`, floored at unit cost, reversible). Adding an action = one registry entry + its primitive, never a bespoke service — this is the shape that replaces per-action shapers. Pure contract + `validateActionIntent` (structural; the primitive still re-checks safety at execution), unit-tested; **unwired** — the plan-pipeline emit + the intent→preview resolution land next. `app/lib/actions/action-intent.server.js`.
- Added the **action-execution ledger** (`action_executions` + `action_execution_writes`) — the execution record for the typed action-primitive layer (the Action Capability Registry), converged with the execution-contract owner. **Generic across primitives** (an `action_type` column, not a per-action table): one row per action run — the approved preview, the autonomy decision (merchant setting / resolved mode / structural eligibility), and the Observe→Learn outcome — plus per-target write rows whose unique `(execution, target, value)` key gives run-scoped idempotency and whose `expected_from` is the compare-and-set + revert source (authoritative prior value; no denormalized reversibility snapshot). **Additive, deploys empty — nothing writes here until an action primitive is wired + flagged on.** `prisma/schema.prisma`, migration `20260730130000_action_execution_ledger`; applied clean to the test DB, `prisma generate` green. Part of the LLM-advice → action-intent → typed-primitive path (founder architecture steer).
- Added the **clearance outcome measurement** (`measureClearanceOutcome`) — the Observe→Learn half of the action loop. Given an applied clearance run (which variants were marked down, and when) plus the sales that followed, it measures whether the action actually _worked_: units moved, cash recovered, and the **effectiveness rate** (share of cleared variants that sold ≥1 unit post-markdown) — counting only post-clearance sales, so a prior sale can't take false credit. This is the substrate of earned autonomy (a merchant whose clearances reliably move stock is one Jefe can act for with less friction) and feeds a memory belief parallel to `business.recommendation_engagement` once the `action_executions` ledger lands (being agreed with the execution-contract owner). Pure + deterministic (applied changes + line items as args), **unwired, no external write, no schema change** — the measurement core built ahead of its loader, per the usual pure-first pattern. `app/lib/actions/clearance-outcome.server.js` + `tests/clearance-outcome.test.mjs` (5 cases); typecheck green. Part of clearance go-live (founder-directed: "did the clearance move the stock").

- **Standalone Slack "Connect" fixed + a "skip setup" escape hatch.** Two onboarding-funnel fixes. **(1) Slack Connect on standalone (#19):** the Slack "Connect" CTA failed on `app.mynamejefe.com` — it POSTed with an App Bridge id-token `Bearer`, and `shopify.idToken()` rejects when there's no App Bridge (standalone), which aborted the OAuth start and surfaced a "failed" card. Now the Bearer is skipped when the id-token can't be obtained; the same-origin POST still carries the host-scoped cookie session, which `channel.slack.start` already accepts (embedded is 100% unchanged — it still sends the Bearer). **(2) Skip onboarding** (founder-requested): a subtle **"Skip setup — go to Jefe →"** button, shown **only once Shopify is connected** (skipping before Connect would land on an empty home), that marks the shop onboarded (`skipOnboarding`, `completedSource: "skipped"`) and drops a returning/experienced merchant straight on the home instead of the full Connect→Channels→Insights→Goals→Plan flow — distinct from the existing per-step "Skip for now →" (which only advances one step). `app/routes/app._index.tsx`; service fn `skipOnboarding` in `onboarding.server.js`. Full gate green (362/362); coordinated with chat 7 (they're not touching `app._index.tsx` tonight — the decomposition extractions are monitored-window work).

- **Daily Home honesty pass — the default screen no longer presents fake things as real.** The live default post-onboarding screen (`daily-home.tsx`, on by default) padded real data with ~15 dead controls + fabricated data shown as live — a genuine **trust + safety issue** and a Product Truth violation (nothing fake may present as working-but-real). Fixed in two passes. **Dead controls → inert "· soon":** the primary **"Approve"/"Decline" CTAs** (Brief + Queue), the fake **"Ask Jefe anything" composer** (→ an honest line pointing to the real channels: reply to a Jefe email / message in Slack), the Settings **Data** actions incl. a fake destructive **"Delete everything"** (→ inert + "to remove Jefe now, uninstall from Shopify admin"), plus Invite / Connect / Create key / the design-partner Record·Write·Book-a-slot. **Fabricated data → honest:** the fake "Detected on your store: Klaviyo, Recharge, ShipStation", the fabricated "Three times this week Jefe had to guess at your margin" stat, the "enforced server-side" team-roles/spend-limits claim, and the `claude mcp add jefe --url mcp.jefe.app` command for a server that doesn't exist — all reframed as coming-soon. Real content — store metrics, the recommendation, computed Horizon dates, and the **Memory beliefs** — is untouched (a shared `inertControl` + `SoonTag` do the gating; the unused `MIC_ICON` was removed). `app/components/daily-home.tsx`. Coordinated: chat 4 (owns the file's Phase-2 memory hosting) green-lit; chat 7's perf moves are in `app._index.tsx`, not this file. The proper fix for the gated surfaces (real settings/guardrails, team, integrations) is the follow-up build bucket.

- **Fixed live 404s from the welcome email.** The now-live welcome email linked "Change my limits" → `/settings/guardrails` and "Email preferences" → `/settings/notifications` — neither route exists, so both 404'd from the inbox. Interim honest repoint (in `welcome.server.js`, no template change): email-preferences → the signed one-click `/e/unsubscribe` (the real email control), change-my-limits → the app deep link (where guardrails will live). No more dead links from a live email; a real settings/guardrails + notification-preferences surface is the proper follow-up (pairs with the Daily Home Settings-tab honesty pass). `app/lib/email/welcome.server.js`, `tests/email.test.mjs`.

- **LLM cost metering completed across all 7 call sites — honest margin.** The cost ledger (`llm_usage_event`) captured only 3 of 7 `createLlmProvider` call sites, so the ops panel's LLM cost — and the margin that subtracts it — was undercounted (margin overstated). The remaining four now pass a `usage:` context: **`conversation`** (the Slack-driven message→operation path, likely the biggest untracked spend), **`store_understanding`**, **`insight_correction`**, and **`goals_document`**. Portfolio and per-merchant LLM cost + margin are now complete end-to-end. Pricing is still placeholder (`pricing.server.js`) until real Gemini rates land, so treat absolute costs as indicative. `app/lib/merchant-memory/conversation.server.js` + `store-understanding.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-insights/service.server.js`, `app/lib/merchant-goals/service.server.js`.

- **Ops panel: uninstall-reason breakdown + portfolio economics (obs #17, #20).** The internal panel (`admin.mynamejefe.com`) overview gains: **"Why they left"** — the latest win-back-feedback reason per shop (consumes the PII-free `shop_uninstall_feedback` event; empty until the farewell email starts sending feedback links, then it self-populates); **"LLM cost by feature · 7d"** (which features drive inference spend — the provider-cost/margin lever); and a coverage-gated **"Margin by client"** table (net revenue − COGS − LLM cost, flagged _indicative_ where COGS coverage < 70%, each row links to the drill-down). The merchant drill-down header shows the shop's stated reason when churned. All read-only SQL, validated against prod before ship. `apps/ops/server.mjs`. Also extracted the panel's pure render helpers to `apps/ops/format.mjs` with 12 `node --test` cases — `apps/ops` previously had zero tests (`apps/ops/tests/format.test.mjs`).

- **`/health` worker-liveness + dependency diagnostics (obs #14).** `/health` now reports, non-gating (never affects the `/ready` gate that Railway uses, so a flaky probe can't recycle a healthy instance): the **backfill worker's liveness** (`buildWorkerHealth` — ok / stale / starting / disabled + last-tick age, via an in-memory heartbeat the worker writes each tick; a wedged loop was previously invisible) and cheap **dependency flags** (`buildDependencyHealth` — email / Slack / LLM configured, env-only, no network), alongside the request-latency percentiles. `app/services/deployment-health.server.js`, `app/lib/observability/heartbeat.server.js`, `app/routes/health.tsx`; `tests/health-diagnostics.test.mjs`.

- **CI schema/migration drift guard (obs #4).** `ci.yml` gains a `prisma migrate diff` step after `migrate deploy` that fails when `schema.prisma` has changes with no matching migration (which would break a fresh `migrate deploy`). Shipped **non-blocking** (`continue-on-error`) until a first green run confirms no pre-existing definition-level drift, then it flips to a hard gate. An audit found no app drift (only a rogue `waitlist_signups` marketing table, not app-referenced — out of scope).

- **Onboarding resume-point persistence hardened + covered.** `recordFurthestOnboardingStep` (the writer behind "resume where you left off") is now **self-guarding**: it reads the shop's onboarding metadata _fresh_ inside the call — not the caller's snapshot — and only ever **advances** the furthest step. A backward `?step=` nav, a stale snapshot, or a future caller that forgets to guard can no longer regress a returning merchant's resume point or clobber a concurrent metadata write (e.g. the plan-completion's `completedStep`); an unknown step is a no-op. Belt-and-suspenders with the existing call-site check — and deliberately robust while `app._index` (which holds that call site) is being decomposed. Also adds the **first tests for `onboarding.server.js`** (`tests/onboarding-server.test.mjs`: advance / monotonic / unknown-step / missing-shop / completion-preserves-metadata). No schema change; happy-path behaviour unchanged. Full gate green (361/361 with DB). `app/services/onboarding.server.js`.

- **Win-back follow-through: reinstall reset + dispatch health event.** The win-back guard (`winback_email_sent_at`) is now cleared on (re)install (`afterAuth`) so a merchant who leaves, returns, and leaves again gets a _fresh_ farewell — the win-back is per churn _event_, not once per shop lifetime (`clearWinBackGuard`, a conditional no-op write on a first install). And each win-back dispatch now emits a **PII-free `email_sent` activity event** (`kind: winback`, delivered/disabled — no recipient) so email sends/failures show up in the ops feed (the observability the new path needs). Still dark behind `ENABLE_WINBACK_EMAIL`. `app/lib/email/winback.server.js`, `app/shopify.server.ts`; covered by new tests in `tests/winback-email.test.mjs`. Full gate green (358/358 with DB).
- Added the **changelog watcher** — a scheduled job (run from the worker tick in Railway infra, never a local routine) that announces new `CHANGELOG.md` entries to Slack. It parses the changelog into per-entry records and, on each check, posts entries it hasn't seen before to `CHANGELOG_WEBHOOK_URL` (or `ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL` → #jefe-slack). **Durable dedup, so a deploy never re-announces:** every entry is keyed by a content hash and recorded in a new `changelog_announcements` table (migration `20260730120000_changelog_announcements`) — unlike the daily-digest's in-memory guard, which re-posts on every restart. **Seed, don't flood:** on the very first run the table is empty, so the entire current changelog is recorded as `seeded` **without** posting — turning the watcher on doesn't dump the backlog into the channel; only entries shipped in a _later_ deploy are announced. Throttled (re-parses at most every 15 min), capped (≤8 announcements/run as a mis-parse backstop), and non-throwing (a parse/DB/webhook hiccup can never break the worker). Opt-in via `ENABLE_CHANGELOG_WATCHER=true`. The `changelog_announcements` table is also the durable source a public-facing changelog page can render later (that page needs curated/merchant-friendly copy — deliberately a follow-up, not raw dev entries). `app/services/changelog/changelog-watcher.server.js`, wired into `shopify-backfill-worker.server.js`; covered by `tests/changelog-watcher.test.mjs` (pure parsing + DB-backed seed→announce). Full gate green (356/356 with DB), lint green.
- Added the **win-back (farewell) email** — the churn-side counterpart to the Day-0 welcome, triggered by the Shopify `app/uninstalled` webhook. It acknowledges the exit with dignity (no guilt-trip), makes **one** honest feedback ask — "What made you uninstall?" as four one-tap options (`too_early` / `no_value` / `too_complex` / `broke`) plus an open "just reply" — and leaves a soft door open to reconnect. Same design system as the welcome (cream card on greige, Georgia display, terracotta accent, navy CTA, "— Jefe" close), 100% inline-CSS. Each feedback option is an **HMAC-signed one-tap link** (binds shop + recipient email **hash** + one locked reason code; never plaintext, can't be forged to mis-attribute a reason) to a new public `/e/feedback` endpoint, which records a **PII-free `shop_uninstall_feedback` lifecycle event** (append-only, same event-log pattern as churn capture — readback takes the latest per shop). Recipient is resolved from the persisted Shopify `Session` (owner email + name), read **before** install teardown. Idempotent (one send per shop via a new additive nullable `shops.winback_email_sent_at` guard, migration `20260730100000_winback_email_sent_guard`) and **doubly gated**: the existing `ENABLE_EMAIL` switch **and** a new win-back-specific `ENABLE_WINBACK_EMAIL` kill-switch. **Shipped inert / dark:** with `ENABLE_WINBACK_EMAIL` unset (the default), the uninstall trigger is a hard no-op — it resolves no recipient, claims no guard, and never contacts Resend — so all the code (template, tokens, endpoint, webhook hook) is in prod but **no farewell email leaves until a human flips the flag**. `app/lib/email/winback.server.js`, `app/lib/email/feedback.server.js`, `app/routes/e.feedback.tsx`, `app/lib/email/templates/jefe-winback.html`; webhook hook in `webhooks.server.js`; covered by `tests/winback-email.test.mjs` (11 tests incl. DB-backed idempotency + dark-by-default); full gate green (348/348 with DB), typecheck + lint green. Follow-ups (with Matt): flip `ENABLE_WINBACK_EMAIL` on after a review of a live render; reset the guard on reinstall so a re-churn re-sends; host the header lockup asset.
- Added **`business.discount_depth.trailing_90d`** to Merchant Memory — how much of the store's pre-discount revenue is given away in discounts (total discounts ÷ gross), plus the **share of orders that carried any discount**. A margin-leak signal that complements the margin / clearance beliefs: heavy discounting can mask thin real margins. Shop base currency, deterministic; registry def (129→130); new fixture test. Belief suite green in isolation (36/36) + typecheck green (an unrelated cross-file email-test isolation flake in the full suite is being flagged to that lane — not from this change).
- Added **`products.dead_stock.trailing_90d`** to Merchant Memory — the trapped-cash _fact_ behind the clearance action. Active products with **inventory on hand but no sales in 90 days**, each with its **trapped capital** (units on hand × unit cost, where cost is known — the real capital tied up, never guessed), plus the total and the headline product. Complements the existing no-sale _count_ with the value + the named products, and it surfaces through the existing memory/insight surfaces (no new UI needed). Deterministic; registry def (128→129); new fixture test; full gate green (349/349), typecheck green.
- Added the **dead-stock clearance decision engine** — the "brain" of Jefe's first executable action (founder-directed; the action frontier begins). `buildDeadStockClearanceProposal` identifies dead stock (active-product variants with inventory on hand, no sales in 90 days, and a _known_ unit cost) and `sizeClearanceMarkdowns` sizes a safe markdown for each — **floored at unit cost, so Jefe never proposes selling below what a product cost.** That floor is the uniquely-Jefe part: it needs the per-variant margin / `unitCost` foundation, so a generic tool can't do it. Variants already at/below cost are excluded and only counted (clearing them is a loss — a merchant judgement). Output is a **read-only proposal** (trapped capital, suggested prices, projected recovery) — **no external write**; the typed adapter that _executes_ an approved proposal is separate and gated. `app/lib/actions/dead-stock-clearance.server.js`; covered by pure-sizing + DB-loader tests; full gate green (348/348), typecheck green. The decision half of belief → decision → (typed action) → outcome.

### Security

- **Ops panel re-gated — always requires the password + logs every access (App Store pre-submit gate).** `apps/ops` had an `OPS_PUBLIC=true` escape hatch serving the internal panel (merchant margins, churn, per-client economics) with **no login** — and it was **on in prod**. Removed the flag entirely so `isAuthed` (Basic auth vs `OPS_PASSWORD`) _always_ gates; `/healthz` stays pre-auth so the uptime probe is unaffected. Added a **PII-safe access log** — one structured JSON line per request, granted _and_ denied: source IP, method, path, the `shop` filter, outcome, timestamp — **never the panel's data or the password** — to stdout for Railway's drain. Together these let the founder truthfully attest "limit staff access" + "log access to PII" on the App Store Data-protection form. `apps/ops/server.mjs`, `apps/ops/format.mjs` (`formatAccessLog` + test, 13 ops tests). Deploys via `railway up --service jefe-ops`; the panel needs the password afterwards. Coordinated with chat 10 (architecture).

- **PII/secret redaction hardened at the `redact()` layer — Errors no longer vanish, embedded secrets no longer leak.** Two gaps surfaced auditing the scrubber that cleans context for _both_ structured logs and Sentry. **(1)** A raw `Error` redacted to `{}` — `message`/`stack` are non-enumerable so `Object.keys()` missed them; the logger's top-level `serializeError` covered the common `err`/`error` case, but an Error nested anywhere else (`{ result: { err } }`, `[{ error }]`) collapsed to empty, silently losing it. `redact()` now serialises any `Error` at any depth (name/message/stack + own fields, cyclic-cause safe) and scrubs the free-text inside. **(2)** Only emails were scrubbed from free text, so a token embedded in an error message/URL leaked; now high-confidence secret shapes — Shopify (`shpat_/shpca_/shppa_/shpss_`), Stripe (`sk|rk|pk_live|test_`, `whsec_`), GitHub (`ghp_…`) and `Bearer/Basic/Token` auth values — are scrubbed to `[redacted-secret]`, patterns kept narrow so legit strings (shop domains, `gid://…`, SKUs) are untouched. Defense-in-depth for the safety-critical primitive; +6 tests (14 total). `app/lib/observability/redact.server.js`, `tests/observability-redact.test.mjs`.

### Fixed

- **Duplicate `app/uninstalled` no longer leaves a churned shop `active` (bug).** A retried uninstall webhook hit `ensureShopifyTenant` — which reactivates an "uninstalled" shop back to "active" — _before_ the dedupe short-circuit returned "duplicate", so a redelivery left the shop stuck `active`, corrupting the churn signal and the retention/GDPR posture. `app/uninstalled` is now handled _before_ the dedupe check: churn is captured once (gated on `created`) and `markShopifyInstallInactive` is **always** re-asserted (idempotent). Also adds the additive nullable `Shop.uninstalledAt` (migration `20260729130000_shop_uninstalled_at`), set on inactivation. `app/lib/ingestion/shopify/webhooks.server.js`; regression test in `tests/shopify-ingestion.test.mjs`.

- **Post-deploy alert noise cut: streaming aborts + startup DB blips no longer page.** Benign, deploy-correlated signals that were paging #jefe-slack now log WARN instead: streaming render errors from client aborts / premature-close / ECONNRESET (`isBenignStreamError`), readiness/health/worker DB-connection blips within a post-deploy grace window (`READINESS_ALERT_GRACE_SECONDS`, default 60s, while the Prisma engine + Neon pool warm), and bot `POST /` 4xx. Real failures after the grace window still page. `app/lib/observability/stream-errors.server.js`, `app/services/deployment-health.server.js`, `app/entry.server.tsx`.

- **Backfill loop pages only on a SUSTAINED failure, not a single transient blip.** The Shopify backfill loop retries every tick, so one failed tick — a Neon connection drop, a deploy warmup, or a Prisma engine blip like `Response from the Engine was empty` — self-heals on the next tick and no longer pages #jefe-slack; it logs WARN with the failure streak. Only once the consecutive-failure streak reaches `BACKFILL_LOOP_PAGE_AFTER` (default 3 ≈ 45s at the 15s tick) does it page (ERROR + `worker_error` event) — a genuine sustained outage. Replaces the worker's earlier grace-window + Prisma-error-string classifier (which let unrecognised transient variants through and page), and is **error-agnostic** so no new engine-error string can slip past. `app/services/shopify-backfill-worker.server.js`, `app/services/deployment-health.server.js` (`shouldPageOnWorkerError` now takes the streak; the unused `isTransientDbConnectionError` removed); `tests/deployment-health-grace.test.mjs`.

## 2026-07-29

### Added

- Added **intent-capture** — the first increment of the demand-derived action ontology (founder-blessed direction; see `docs/action-ontology-and-autonomy.md`). When a merchant asks Jefe to _do_ something it can't yet ("reorder my bestseller", "email my VIPs"), the conversation can't resolve it into a memory operation and lands as `clarificationRequired`; Jefe now logs that unresolved ask as a **PII-safe `merchant_intent_unfulfilled` activity event** (topic `intent`, via the best-effort `track()` — never affects the reply). This is the _seed corpus_ for the recursive ontology: the raw signal of what merchants want Jefe to do but can't yet, to be mined later by frequency × value to decide which actions to build. **Observation-only** — no external writes, no schema change (reuses the activity-events stream), no autonomy change. `app/lib/merchant-memory/conversation.server.js` (`buildUnfulfilledIntentEvent`); covered by a new unit test; full gate green, typecheck green. Next: an LLM-classified `action_request` operation type (richer than the clarification proxy) + offline clustering that turns the corpus into candidate ontology entries.
- Added **margin by region** to Merchant Memory — `business.margin_by_region.trailing_90d`: gross margin per **destination country** (revenue − COGS over the cost-covered share of each region's revenue). The **profitability** half of the store-split / international-expansion decision — `revenue_by_region` says _where_ the revenue is; this says whether it's _profitable_ there (a market can be big on revenue but thin on margin once the regional cost mix lands). **Doubly coverage-gated:** on known destination country (≥70% of window revenue) _and_, per region, on cost coverage (a region's margin is stated only where ≥70% of its revenue has a known cost — never guessed). Reuses the `shippingCountry` column + `Variant.unitCost` already in place; **no schema change**. Derivation in `shopify-derivations.server.js`, registry def (127→128); covered by a new fixture test (GB 60% vs US 30%, proving regional margin genuinely differs); full gate green (322/322 with DB), typecheck green.
- Added **revenue by region** to Merchant Memory — `business.revenue_by_region.trailing_90d`: revenue split by **destination country** over the trailing 90 days (top countries with their share, in shop base currency). Jefe now captures each order's shipping/billing `country_code` into a new additive `Order.shippingCountry` column (migration `20260729160000_order_shipping_country`) and derives the split, **coverage-gated** — it publishes only when ≥70% of window revenue carries a known country (older orders lack it until re-backfilled), never a split on data it doesn't have. This is the geo signal behind the decision international operators are weighing right now ("is my US volume enough to justify a separate US store / retail presence?") and it's core to the international-DTC ICP. Derivation in `shopify-derivations.server.js`, ingestion in `canonical.server.js`, registry def (126→127); covered by a new fixture test in `tests/merchant-memory.test.mjs`; full gate green (321/321 with DB). Follow-up: a coarse region rollup (UK / EU / US / ROW) and a domestic-vs-international headline once the shop's own country is read.
- Added the **cost-sheet ingestion engine** — the first net-new modality of the multi-modal memory feed. A merchant's uploaded costs spreadsheet is parsed **deterministically** (SKU + cost columns detected by header aliases; currency symbols and thousands separators handled) into `{sku, cost}` entries, then **gap-fills** `Variant.unitCost` for matched variants that don't already have a cost — directly raising `products.cost_coverage` and unlocking the gross-margin belief for merchants who won't fill cost-per-item one product at a time in Shopify. Because a costs sheet is _structured_ input it's treated as a deterministic fact (no LLM inference); if the SKU or cost column can't be confidently detected it returns "needs confirmation" rather than guessing. v1 is **gap-fill only** — it never overwrites an existing Shopify-observed cost (override + cost provenance is a documented follow-up). Engine in `app/lib/ingestion/merchant-cost-sheet.server.js` (`parseCostSheet` + `ingestMerchantCostRows`), covered by `tests/merchant-cost-sheet.test.mjs` (deterministic-parser cases + a DB-gated apply test). Not yet merchant-reachable: the upload surface that feeds it (row contract = an array of `{header: value}` objects) is the memory-feed UI in progress.

- **Standalone web-app access (out-of-iframe): auth foundation + OAuth bridge.** `app.mynamejefe.com` can serve the SAME `/app` experience outside the Shopify iframe — shop-domain OAuth (offline) → signed standalone session → same shell, **embedded flow 100% unchanged**. A dedicated `@shopify/shopify-api` instance scoped to the standalone host (`SHOPIFY_APP_URL` + embedded untouched) runs the library's tested OAuth on `/standalone/auth` + `/standalone/callback`; a host-primary seam (`authenticateAppRequest`) renders `/app` embedded (`authenticate.admin`) or standalone (`unauthenticated.admin(shop)`). The session cookie is httpOnly/Secure/SameSite=Lax, host-scoped, and carries only `{shop, iat, exp}` — the offline token never leaves the server. Existing-merchant sign-in only (an unknown shop is routed to the managed install). No new scopes; no schema change. Merged **inert** — activates once the callback URL is whitelisted and the landing CTA is flipped. Covered by 28 standalone auth unit tests.
- Added the first **Observe→Learn** signal to Merchant Memory — `business.recommendation_engagement.all_time`: how the merchant engages with Jefe's recommendations, from the plan-recommendation review outcomes already captured on the model (accepted / rejected / completed), as counts plus acceptance and completion rates. This is the first step onto the earned-autonomy ramp: a merchant who consistently accepts and completes a class of recommendation is one Jefe can eventually act for with less friction — memory now _records_ that engagement instead of recommending and forgetting. Gated on ≥3 recommendations; read into the derivation context with a guard so nothing depends on the plan subsystem being present. Derivations in `shopify-derivations.server.js` + registry def (125→126); covered by a new fixture test; full gate green (283/283 with DB). **No schema change** — the disposition fields (`reviewStatus`, `acceptedAt`, `completedAt`, `successSignal`) already exist. Follow-ups: per-recommendation-type engagement, and closing the loop by measuring each recommendation's `successSignal` outcome over time.
- Added **seasonality** to Merchant Memory — `business.peak_sales_month.all_time`: which calendar month is the store's sales peak, from revenue aggregated by month-of-year across all stored history (shop timezone, shop base currency), with the full 12-month breakdown and each month's share. Gated on ≥20 dated orders spanning ~12 months, so it only claims a season once there's a year to judge — the second belief (after year-over-year) to draw on the extended 24-month history window. This is the "plan for your BFCM/Christmas peak" signal. Derivations in `shopify-derivations.server.js` + registry def (124→125); covered by a new fixture test (18 months, November peak); full gate green (282/282 with DB). No schema change. Follow-up: seasonal peak _week_ and a per-category seasonal calendar.
- Added **attribute-level product performance** to Merchant Memory — two deterministic `products` beliefs that answer "which categories/brands drive my revenue": **`products.revenue_by_product_type.trailing_90d`** (revenue grouped by Shopify **product type**, top types with revenue + share) and **`products.revenue_by_vendor.trailing_90d`** (revenue grouped by **vendor**/brand). This is the "analyse by category" view operators merchandise by, and it needs **no new ingestion** — `product_type` and `vendor` were already stored, just not read into the derivation context. In shop base currency; products with no type/vendor are pooled as **unattributed** revenue (not dropped) so the shares stay honest about coverage. Derivations in `shopify-derivations.server.js` + registry defs (122→124); covered by a new fixture test; full gate green (281/281 with DB). No schema change. Follow-up: collection-level performance (needs collection-membership ingestion).
- Added **gap-driven open questions** to Merchant Memory — memory now generates its own targeted questions for the gaps only the merchant can fill, instead of relying on two static seeds. It inspects the belief state and raises a question when, e.g., **product costs are missing or thin** (cost coverage < 70% or no margin belief → "Jefe can see cost prices for only X% of your products… add cost-per-item so it can track margin") or **several active products haven't sold in 90 days** (→ "treat these as discontinued, seasonal, or still worth promoting?"). Crucially it's a **closed loop**: each question is keyed and reconciled on every conversation load — a gap that gets filled (costs added → coverage rises) **retracts** its question automatically, a gap that returns re-opens it, and a question the merchant already answered is never re-asked. This is what lets memory actively reduce its own uncertainty and feed the correction loop rather than waiting to be corrected. Deterministic generation in `conversation.server.js` (`buildGapDrivenOpenQuestions` + reconciliation); covered by a new unit test; full gate green (280/280 with DB). No schema change (uses the existing open-questions model). Follow-up: more gap rules (sales-channel coverage, returns spikes) as the belief set grows.
- Added **inventory velocity + reorder signals** to Merchant Memory — two deterministic `inventory` beliefs that join recent sell-rate to current stock: **`inventory.at_risk_stockout_count.trailing_30d`** (how many selling products will run out within **21 days** at the current 30-day sell-rate) and **`inventory.low_cover_products.trailing_30d`** (the actionable **reorder list** — the products closest to running out, each with its days of cover, units in stock and daily sell-rate). Days of cover = available units ÷ (units sold in 30 days ÷ 30). Honest by construction: missing inventory is **unknown, never zero** (only products with at least one tracked variant are evaluated), and slow sellers (<3 units in the window) are excluded so a one-off sale can't fake a velocity. This is the "reorder before you stock out" signal an autonomous manager acts on — and one of the highest-demand asks from the operator channel. Derivations in `shopify-derivations.server.js` + registry defs (120→122); covered by a new fixture test; full gate green (279/279 with DB). No schema change (uses existing inventory levels + line items). Follow-ups: per-variant reorder points and supplier lead-time-aware thresholds.
- Added **customer memory** to Merchant Memory — three deterministic, PII-safe beliefs derived from the hashed customer identities (counts, shares and aggregate money only; never an identity): **`customers.repeat_revenue_share.all_time`** (share of revenue from repeat customers — the "returning customers drive X% of sales" signal, distinct from the existing repeat _customer_ rate, which is share of _customers_), **`customers.average_lifetime_spend.all_time`** (average customer lifetime spend, split by repeat vs one-time — an LTV proxy that makes the value of retention visible), and **`customers.top_customer_revenue_share.all_time`** (revenue concentration across the top 10 customers — a concentration-risk signal). All in **shop base currency** (each customer's `totalSpend` is aggregated from `currentTotalPriceSet.shopMoney` at ingest and deduped by order id, so they're summable across a multi-currency store) and **coverage-gated on ≥10 known customers with recorded spend** — never a guess on a thin base. Customer lifetime value is one of the dimensions merchants actually think in ("my repeat customers are half my revenue") and was the biggest missing fidelity dimension. Derivations in `shopify-derivations.server.js` + registry defs in `deterministic-belief-registry.server.js` (117→120); covered by a new fixture test in `tests/merchant-memory.test.mjs` including a PII-leak assertion; full gate green (278/278 with DB). **No schema change** — the `CustomerIdentity` aggregates already exist. Follow-ups: windowed new-vs-returning revenue (needs per-order sequence) and cohort-adjusted LTV.

- **Client-side error capture (Sentry) — roadmap #3.** Browser errors (render errors caught by the root `ErrorBoundary`, plus uncaught errors / unhandled rejections) now report to Sentry via a new client entry (`app/entry.client.tsx`) + `app/lib/observability/sentry.client.ts`, closing the half server-side Sentry couldn't see. Inert unless `VITE_SENTRY_DSN` is set (now set on the jefe service; a DSN is a public client identifier); PII posture mirrors the server (`sendDefaultPii` off, request cookies/body/headers dropped).

- **Churn capture on uninstall — roadmap #11.** The `app/uninstalled` webhook now emits a PII-free `shop_uninstalled` activity event (topic `lifecycle`) with a snapshot of how far the shop got before leaving — tenure, onboarding/backfill/goals progress, COGS coverage, and order/product/belief counts — captured _before_ teardown flips status and deletes sessions. Event-only by design (no schema change). `app/services/analytics/churn.server.js`; `tests/analytics-churn.test.mjs`.

- **Ops panel: per-merchant drill-down, margin, and metrics — roadmap #5, #10, #6, #7.** `admin.mynamejefe.com` gains a per-merchant view (event timeline + LLM cost + 14-day activity/cost sparklines) and a coverage-gated **unit-economics** row — net revenue, COGS, and margin (revenue − COGS − LLM cost) — shown "indicative" when unit-cost coverage is thin rather than falsely precise. The overview adds Active-7d, LLM p50/p95 latency, job success-rate, errors-24h (warn), and activity/cost trend sparklines. All read-only SQL, validated against prod before ship. `apps/ops/server.mjs`.

- **Request-latency percentiles on `/health` — roadmap #7.** An in-memory sampler (`app/lib/observability/perf.server.js`) records request durations and exposes p50/p95/p99 on `/health` (per-instance, live); LLM-call duration percentiles are surfaced durably in the ops panel from the usage ledger. `tests/observability-perf.test.mjs`.

- **Opt-in event-log retention — roadmap #9.** `app/services/analytics/retention.server.js` prunes `activity_events` / `llm_usage_event` beyond a configurable window so the tables stay bounded — gated on `ENABLE_EVENT_RETENTION` (off by default; premature at current volume) and run from the worker's existing daily guard, no new scheduler. `tests/analytics-retention.test.mjs`.
- Added **recent revenue trend** to Merchant Memory — `business.revenue_trend.trailing_180d`: is the store **growing / flat / declining** right now (revenue in the last 90 days vs the immediately prior 90, ±10% threshold, with both figures). The sequential complement to year-over-year — it catches a recent turn (up or down) that a year-ago comparison can miss. Deterministic; covered by a new fixture test; full gate green (262/262). No schema change.
- Added **year-over-year revenue growth** to Merchant Memory — `business.yoy_revenue_growth.trailing_90d`: revenue in the trailing 90 days vs the same 90 days a year ago (% change, with both figures).
